# Idea backlog

Generated by `tools/Update-Backlog.ps1` from [the idea sheet](https://docs.google.com/spreadsheets/d/1nd_oWjYyphCcjt5F0SrstpkMK_UzDdKwj0U1UC3FHG0/edit)
and the research in `docs/research/idea-research.json` (game 1.0.16, researched 2026-09-28).
Do not edit by hand: change the sheet or the research data, then re-run the script.

**Feasibility:** trivial → easy → medium → hard → very-hard. **Who needs it:** Client = only the player who wants it;
Server = the host/dedicated server; Both = server and every player; Depends = see details. **Assets:** needs custom
models, textures, animations or sounds. **Existing mods:** how much of it already exists on Nexus/Thunderstore
(details and links per idea below; the game-systems chapters are in `docs/game/`). **Status:** the sheet's Status
(idea, cancelled...), or a link to the mod once one in `src/` implements the idea (in development or released).

## Combat

### Crossbow revamp

> Stays loaded

- **Scope:** QoL · **Feasibility:** easy · **Who needs it:** Client · **Custom assets:** no · **Status:** [Crossbow Stays Loaded 0.1.0](../src/Combat/Crossbow.StaysLoaded) (in development)
- **Approach:** Keep a per-item flag in ItemData.m_customData (e.g. 'VM.CrossbowLoaded'), which is persisted in inventories, chests, drops and saves and ignored by vanilla. A postfix on the private Player.SetWeaponLoaded sets the flag when the weapon is non-null. A postfix on Attack.OnAttackTrigger clears it only when the shot actually fired, i.e. the character is no longer loaded. A prefix on the private Player.UpdateWeaponLoading instantly calls SetWeaponLoaded(weapon) and skips the reload queue when the newly equipped crossbow is flagged. Vanilla unequip paths keep nulling m_weaponLoaded harmlessly, and the ZDO WeaponLoaded bool keeps visuals correct for all players.
- **Hooks:** `Player.SetWeaponLoaded`, `Player.UpdateWeaponLoading`, `Attack.OnAttackTrigger`, `Attack.GetWeapon`, `Player.ResetLoadedWeapon`, `Humanoid.UnequipItem`, `ItemDrop.ItemData.GetTooltip`
- **Risks:** Pre-loading several crossbows enables instant swap volleys, so add a config option to remember only the last loaded one. Ammo is consumed at fire time, so there is no ammo dupe. The target methods are private (AccessTools or a publicized assembly) and may be renamed by game patches. Any m_requiresReload weapon from content mods is affected, which is generic and intended.
- **Game systems:** [docs/game/combat.md](game/combat.md)
- **Existing mods:** full

  | Mod | Status | Notes |
  |---|---|---|
  | [SaveCrossbowState (Azumatt)](https://thunderstore.io/c/valheim/p/Azumatt/SaveCrossbowState/) | v1.0.2, updated 2025-08-29, DEPRECATED, no reason given (1.0: unknown) | Exactly the feature: keeps the loaded state across weapon swap or unequip, and also covers Dundr since v1.0.1. Client-only, no config. About 152K downloads. No public repo found. |
  | [FastArbalest (Neobotics)](https://thunderstore.io/c/valheim/p/Neobotics/FastArbalest/) | v1.0.0, updated 2026-09-11 (1.0: yes) | Reload rate, pausable reload, recoil. 'Sling Loaded' keeps the bolt only while the crossbow is hidden or you are swimming; unequipping or swapping still unloads it. |
  | [BetterCrossbows (xtavim)](https://thunderstore.io/c/valheim/p/xtavim/BetterCrossbows/) | v1.1.0, updated 2026-09-09 (1.0: likely) | Reload time, move while reloading, stamina toggle, ServerSync. Tells users to add SaveCrossbowState for this feature. Source: github.com/xtavim/BetterCrossbows. |
  | [Crossbow Reload Tweaked (Crbakc)](https://thunderstore.io/c/valheim/p/Crbakc/Crossbow_Reload_Tweaked/) | v1.1.1, 2025-06-01 (1.0: unknown) | Reload, movement and stamina tuning while reloading. |

- **Inspiration:** Vanilla: Player keeps the loaded weapon as an ItemData reference (m_weaponLoaded), mirrored to the ZDO (ZDOVars.s_weaponLoaded) through Player.SetWeaponLoaded. Humanoid.UnequipItem calls ResetLoadedWeapon for any weapon. Player.UpdateWeaponLoading queues a reload whenever the equipped weapon is not the loaded one (with eitr drain for Dundr through m_reloadEitrDrain). Ours: a per-item persisted flag (for example ItemData.m_customData) restored on equip, covering every m_requiresReload weapon (crossbows, Dundr, modded), with a clear rule for drop and trade and optional persistence across logout. The gap is simply a maintained, 1.0-verified version.

### Blood stone revamp

> It's hard to interact with the bloodstone mechanic (+,2% damage per missing HP): provide cheap bloodmagic spell to sacrifice life / gain life on hit / lifesteal etc, to have better health management

- **Scope:** Revamp · **Feasibility:** easy · **Who needs it:** Client · **Custom assets:** no · **Status:** idea
- **Approach:** Detect bloodstone weapons from data, not names: any weapon whose m_attack or m_secondaryAttack has m_damageMultiplierPerMissingHP or m_damageMultiplierByTotalHealthMissing above 0. That covers the seven Ashlands Bleeding weapons, probably the Deep North *Gold_BloodLightning set (prefab data, verify), and modded items. Blood rite: a hotkey, read in a Player.Update postfix for the local player, pays a share of current HP with the vanilla blood-magic rule. The rule is mirrored from Attack.GetAttackHealth (up to 33% cheaper with skill; the rite uses the Blood Magic skill factor) and from Attack.Update, which pays UseHealth(min(HP - 1, cost)) so the cost never kills. Character.UseHealth itself only clamps at 0, so the mod applies the HP - 1 cap. The rite costs no eitr, has a short cooldown, raises Blood Magic, and adds a local SE_Stats (or subclass) by reference through SEMan.AddStatusEffect. While the rite is active, a Character.Damage postfix on the attacker's client heals the local player for a share of each enemy hit. The share grows with missing HP and is capped per swing. A Hud.UpdateHealth postfix and the item tooltip show the live '+X% damage' bonus. Optional extras: the first lethal hit during the rite leaves 1 HP (prefix on Character.ApplyDamage for the local player), and a config multiplier for the per-HP bonus, applied in ObjectDB.Awake/CopyOtherDB. No new prefab, item, RPC or asset is needed. A craftable spell item instead of the hotkey would make it medium and require everyone to have the mod.
- **Hooks:** `Attack.ModifyDamage`, `Attack.GetAttackHealth`, `ItemDrop.ItemData.GetTooltip`, `Hud.UpdateHealth`, `Character.UseHealth`, `Character.Heal`, `Player.Update`, `Player.RaiseSkill`, `SEMan.AddStatusEffect`, `SE_Stats`, `Character.Damage`, `BaseAI.IsEnemy`, `Character.ApplyDamage`, `ObjectDB.Awake`, `ObjectDB.CopyOtherDB`
- **Risks:** Lifesteal is estimated from the outgoing hit on the attacker's client, which is also EpicLoot's approach. That happens before the victim owner applies resistances, NG+ enemy armor, difficulty scaling, the backstab bonus and the x2 on staggered targets. The outgoing total also still counts fire, poison and spirit damage that lands later as damage over time. An exact value would need an RPC back from the victim owner, and then everyone would need the mod. Multi-target swings and AoEs call Character.Damage once per target, so cap the heal per attack. Heal only on enemies. BaseAI.IsEnemy is false for tames and for every other player, so PvP needs its own IsPVPEnabled check. It is true for training dummies, and structures are not Characters. Do not reuse vanilla Projectile.m_healthReturn: Projectile.OnHit heals the owner after damaging any destructible, trees and rocks included. EpicLoot LifeSteal/Blood Drinker, JardsAdditions vampirism and WeaponArts Bloodthirst stack with ours and multiply the healing. Food regen every 10 s and health meads keep pushing HP back up. Balance and PvP fairness need a server-synced config. The per-HP values of the Deep North variants are prefab data and must be checked at runtime. It overlaps with the Blood magic XP idea (XP for sacrificing HP), so design the rite's XP together with it. It also overlaps with the Blood trinket, which drops HP to 15% once and, after a 10 s window, heals back what it took over a few seconds. Nothing is clamped in that window, so this lifesteal lifts HP there (and lowers the bloodstone bonus). The rite's m_healthRegenMultiplier = 0 option stops only the food regen tick, not the trinket's heal-back (Character.Heal). The trinket has no 1 HP floor and no Character.ApplyDamage prefix, so this idea's optional last-stand ward is the only one of the two.
- **Related:** Blood magic XP; Gemstone revamp; New magical items based on valheim magic (ward, heal); Adrenaline revamp; Increase base HP/Stamina with stats; Blood trinket
- **Game systems:** [docs/game/combat.md](game/combat.md)
- **Existing mods:** partial

  | Mod | Status | Notes |
  |---|---|---|
  | [JardsAdditions (jard_hu)](https://thunderstore.io/c/valheim/p/jard_hu/JardsAdditions/) | v2.1.0, updated 2025-12-15, tagged Ashlands and Bog Witch updates (1.0: unknown) | The closest match on bloodstone itself. The BloodstoneEffect config picks Damage (default), Vampirism or DamageAndVampirism, so vampirism is off by default. BloodstoneDamagePercent defaults to 0.004, double the vanilla 0.2%. Vampirism returns health after each hit: missing HP x 0.0225 x a weapon factor (higher base damage, so slower weapons heal more) x the world's enemy-damage difficulty factor. Server-enforced config, needs Jötunn, bundled with unrelated vanilla tweaks and PvP protections. It offers no way to lose HP on purpose. About 2.2K downloads, no source link. |
  | [EpicLoot (RandyKnapp)](https://thunderstore.io/c/valheim/p/RandyKnapp/EpicLoot/) | v0.14.13, updated 2026-09-24, tagged Deep North Update (1.0: yes) | Random magic-item effects. LifeSteal heals a share of the outgoing hit, computed on the attacker's side in a shared Character.Damage postfix. Bloodlust turns a weapon's attack stamina cost into a health cost. 'LowHealth' effect variants apply at 30% HP or less by default. Its blood shardstones add Blood Drinker (lifesteal for less max HP), Bloodrage (hits taken stack an outgoing damage bonus) and Blood Base Block (more block power, costs 5% of max HP when a block starts). Driven by loot and sockets, not tied to bloodstone weapons. Needs Jötunn. Source: github.com/RandyKnapp/ValheimMods. About 2.26M downloads. |
  | [WeaponArts (j1gA)](https://thunderstore.io/c/valheim/p/j1gA/WeaponArts/) | v0.14.1, updated 2026-09-26, tested with Valheim 1.0.16 (1.0: yes) | One key (default C) fires an active 'art' chosen by the held weapon type, with cooldowns and a HUD. The one-handed sword's Bloodthirst gives lifesteal from damage dealt, and the battleaxe's Berserk cuts damage taken and regenerates HP. It is the closest match to a hotkey blood rite, but it is tied to weapon type, not to bloodstone, and has no health cost. Install on every player who wants the arts, host recommended. Source: github.com/tbsj1ga/WeaponArtsValheim. AI Generated tag, about 280 downloads. |
  | [BloodMagicCompanion (qwertyzxcv)](https://thunderstore.io/c/valheim/p/qwertyzxcv/BloodMagicCompanion/) | v1.0.0, updated 2026-09-25, compiled against 1.0.16, not playtested (1.0: yes) | Health-only blood magic: each point of base eitr cost becomes 0.5% of current HP, discounted by up to 33% with Blood Magic skill and capped to leave 1 HP. Also gives the caster the Staff of Protection shield-break XP. This makes vanilla blood staffs cheap, but there is no lifesteal and no link to bloodstone. The linked GitHub repo (lhy-loveworld/valheim-blood-magic-companion) holds only the README so far; the README says MIT and AI-assisted. About 33 downloads. |
  | [ValheimLegends (momos3939)](https://thunderstore.io/c/valheim/p/momos3939/ValheimLegends/) | v0.7.10, updated 2026-09-11, 'Compatibility port for Valheim 1.0 (Unity 6)', tagged Deep North Update (1.0: yes) | Class mod. The Berserker sacrifices health for attack speed, movement speed and physical damage. In the original source (github.com/TorannD/ValheimLegends, last pushed 2023), the 18 s Berserk state deals 5% of max health (1 to 15, spirit damage) to the player every 3 s. It also removes the stamina-regen delay and carries a lifesteal share of 20% + 0.2% per level. AI Generated tag, about 4K downloads. |
  | [RacesOfValheim (Nandor)](https://thunderstore.io/c/valheim/p/Nandor/RacesOfValheim/) | v1.5.0, updated 2026-09-28, tagged Deep North Update (1.0: yes) | Playable races. The Aswid (vampire) race gets lifesteal on all damage dealt. It grows from 5% to 35% with boss progression, and the race also has a toggleable life-draining aura. Its blood magic costs health only, with no eitr. A full race overhaul that needs Jötunn. AI Generated tag, about 19.1K downloads. |
  | [SecondaryAttacks (sighsorry)](https://thunderstore.io/c/valheim/p/sighsorry/SecondaryAttacks/) | v1.2.13, updated 2026-09-27, tagged Deep North Update (1.0: yes) | Blood magic staff secondaries. 'Shield Convert' turns the remaining Staff of Protection shield on you and nearby allies into healing, and 'Summon Empower' buffs summons. Its Blood Magic config also tweaks health costs (max-health based costs) and grants Blood Magic XP from health-cost abilities. Nothing for bloodstone weapons. About 12.5K downloads. |
  | [MagicOverhaul (KGvalheim)](https://thunderstore.io/c/valheim/p/KGvalheim/MagicOverhaul/) | v1.5.594, 2022-05-29, deprecated (dead) | 14 classes. DeathKnight converts 3% of damage into HP, Spartan gets passive vampirism at level 100, and Warlock has a rage mode that consumes HP. About 101K downloads. |

- **Inspiration:** Vanilla already has every piece. Attack.m_damageMultiplierPerMissingHP gives 0.2% per missing HP point on the Bleeding weapons (wiki; the value is prefab data). Blood-magic health costs are a % of current HP, reduced by skill, and capped to leave 1 HP. m_attackHealthReturnHit heals a flat amount per character hit. Players already pair the Staff of Protection (60 eitr plus 40% of current HP, and a shield) with bloodstone weapons, and Steam threads ask for heal on hit or life leech on bloodstone weapons. What to borrow: JardsAdditions' vampirism that scales with missing HP and compensates slow weapons, EpicLoot's attacker-side lifesteal and low-health thresholds, WeaponArts' one-key active art with a cooldown and HUD, and Blood Magic Companion's 'leave 1 HP' cost rule. What they get wrong: JardsAdditions adds vampirism (off by default) but predates 1.0 and still offers no way to go low on purpose. EpicLoot, WeaponArts and the class and race mods tie the effect to random loot, a weapon type or a class. Vanilla blood staffs need eitr (Blood Magic Companion removes that) and a weapon swap. Ours is one loop on the bloodstone weapons themselves. A cheap Blood rite pays HP for a short blood buff with no eitr and earns Blood Magic XP. Lifesteal grows as HP falls, so a low-HP fight can be sustained. A visible '+X% damage' readout shows the bonus. Client-only, built from vanilla visuals and rules.

### Boss power revamp

> Boss powers are strong with a large cooldown, This does not necessarily need a revamp but I'm tempted to boost them SLIGHTLY

- **Scope:** Revamp · **Feasibility:** easy · **Who needs it:** Depends · **Custom assets:** no · **Status:** idea
- **Approach:** Keep the vanilla design (activate, buff, cooldown) and make each power slightly stronger with per-power multipliers. Starting values, to tune: duration x 1.2, cooldown x 0.8, effect strength x 1.15 on numeric deltas only (the regen multipliers' part above 1, stamina-use modifiers, speed, carry weight, skill levels, damage percentages). Damage-resistance steps are unchanged by default, because enum steps are not 'slight'. The adrenaline modifier is unchanged (GP_Fader's +100% adrenaline, per the wiki and BossRules' table of the vanilla effects, prefab value unverified, feeds the Adrenaline revamp). The sharing radius stays 10 m unless configured. An ObjectDB.Awake/CopyOtherDB postfix edits StatusEffect.m_ttl, StatusEffect.m_cooldown and the SE_Stats fields of every SE in ObjectDB.m_StatusEffects whose name starts with GP_ (modded powers included, config list), in place on the shared assets. It works from stored originals and reapplies on config change. The cooldown comes from the activator's ObjectDB, because Player.ActivateGuardianPower copies m_guardianSE.m_cooldown into m_guardianPowerCooldown. Duration and values come from each receiver's ObjectDB, because SEMan.Internal_AddStatusEffect looks the hash up locally. Optional radius: Player.ActivateGuardianPower always returns false, so a prefix records whether the power is ready (cooldown 0 and m_guardianSE set) and a postfix, when it was, adds the SE by hash (SEMan.AddStatusEffect with resetTime) to the players between 10 m and the new radius (Player.GetPlayersInRange). Players without the mod receive it too, because the GP effects are vanilla assets. Optional: clamp a saved cooldown that is longer than the new one in a Player.Load postfix. The tooltips read the edited fields (SE_Stats.GetTooltipString), and Player.SetGuardianPower takes the SE from ObjectDB, so no UI patch is needed. Out of scope: passive powers, since the sheet only asks for a slight boost and PassivePowers and ForsakenPowerOverhaul already cover that model. Side: technically depends (the cooldown is the activator's, the buff values each receiver's); ships as Both, so the server refuses players without the mod, sends its multipliers to everyone, and every player in range gets the same boosted power.
- **Hooks:** `ObjectDB.Awake`, `ObjectDB.CopyOtherDB`, `Player.ActivateGuardianPower`, `Player.GetPlayersInRange`, `SEMan.AddStatusEffect`, `Player.Load`, `Player.SetGuardianPower`, `SE_Stats.GetTooltipString`
- **Risks:** Mixed installs happen only when the server allows players without the mod (AllowPlayersWithoutMod): the same shared power is then stronger for receivers who have it. The cooldown is saved in the profile (Player.Save/Load), so a running vanilla cooldown stays until it ends unless it is clamped at load. The GP effect names differ from the stat switch in Player.StartGuardianPower (GP_Fader vs GP_Ashlands), so match effects by name prefix. Whether the Deep North boss has a power in 1.0.16 is unverified (BossRules lists seven, and the SoftRef manifest_extended has only seven GP_* assets). ValheimPlus (guardianBuffDuration/guardianBuffCooldown), ForsakenPowersPlusRemastered, ForsakenPowersRevisited, BossRules, EasyVitals and PassivePowers edit the same effects or fields; the last writer wins, so detect them and step aside. The vanilla durations and cooldowns are prefab data (300 s and 1200 s per third-party dumps). For balance and PvP, keep the defaults small; the server's settings apply to everyone.
- **Related:** Adrenaline revamp
- **Game systems:** [docs/game/combat.md](game/combat.md)
- **Existing mods:** full

About 360K downloads. |

ServerSync. Source: github.com/Turbero/ForsakenPowersPlusRemastered. About 17K downloads. |

Source: github.com/Gerbesh/Forsaken-Powers-revisited (MIT per its README). About 3.5K downloads. |

- **Inspiration:** Vanilla: a power is one SE_Stats (GP_*) with m_ttl (buff length) and m_cooldown. Player.ActivateGuardianPower shares it by hash with players within 10 m, adds 10 adrenaline and starts the cooldown from the activator's copy. Each receiver uses its own ObjectDB copy of the effect, so data edits are enough. What they get wrong: the config mods leave the 'how much' to the user, BossRules' preset changes the powers' character, and EasyVitals doubles the duration without sync. Ours: a tiny data-only mod with a curated preset that is a bit longer, a bit sooner and a bit stronger. It keeps the same model and radius, applies per-power multipliers from stored originals, and the vanilla tooltip shows the new numbers. It ships as Both, with the server's multipliers for everyone. Otherwise, recommend a ValheimPlus or ForsakenPowersPlusRemastered config and skip it.

### Increase base HP/Stamina with stats

> (running jump etc)

- **Scope:** Revamp · **Feasibility:** easy · **Who needs it:** Client · **Custom assets:** no · **Status:** idea
- **Approach:** Postfix the private Player.GetTotalFoodValue (out hp, stamina, eitr) to add capped bonuses from skill levels (Run, Jump, Swim, Blocking) or PlayerProfile stats (DistanceRun, Jumps). Optionally mirror the bonus into m_baseHP so GetBaseFoodHP and the HUD stay consistent. Max HP propagates through the player's own ZDO max_health.
- **Hooks:** `Player.GetTotalFoodValue`, `Player.m_baseHP`, `Player.m_baseStamina`, `Player.GetBaseFoodHP`, `Skills.GetSkillFactor`, `PlayerProfile.GetStat`
- **Risks:** UpdateFood recalculates every second, so setting max HP anywhere else is overwritten. Other food or vitals mods (ValheimPlus food config and similar) patch the same place, so ordering matters. Skill loss on death lowers the bonus. PvP balance needs synced config.
- **Game systems:** [docs/game/combat.md](game/combat.md)
- **Existing mods:** full

Almost exactly this idea. Source repo last pushed 2024. |
  | [StaminaExtended (shudnal)](https://thunderstore.io/c/valheim/p/shudnal/StaminaExtended/) | v1.0.11, updated 2026-09-11 (1.0: likely) | Base stamina scales with skill levels; regen, encumbrance and surface-dependent tweaks. Open source. |

- **Inspiration:** Vanilla: Player.m_baseHP and m_baseStamina (code defaults 25 and 75) plus food via Player.GetTotalFoodValue. The idea is already covered by VitalityRewrite. If we do it, keep it a light, capped bonus from existing skills (Run, Jump, Swim) with no new skill, so it stays subordinate to food.

### Sneak revamp

> Gain XP on sneak attack Make sneak much more efficient when not moving at all

- **Scope:** Revamp · **Feasibility:** easy · **Who needs it:** Both · **Custom assets:** no · **Status:** [Sneak Ambush 0.1.0](../src/Combat/Sneak.Ambush) (in development)
- **Approach:** Vanilla gives Sneak XP only from Player.OnSneaking while the player moves crouched, and the stealth factor (Player.UpdateStealth) ignores movement. XP on sneak attack: a Character.RPC_Damage prefix and postfix on the victim's owner compare the private m_backstabTime, which changes only when the vanilla backstab lands (victim not alerted, backstab bonus above 1, 300 s cooldown per victim, PassiveMobs sight check). With a Player attacker, raise Sneak by a config amount (for example 5, about five seconds of sneaking near enemies) times the victim's max HP divided by a config reference, capped at the top but with no floor so weak creatures pay almost nothing, and halved for ranged hits; directly for the local player or through an RPC on the attacker's ZNetView, which reaches the attacker's own client. Training dummies (Faction.TrainingDummy) pay no XP: they are easy to hit unalerted, and an OFF dummy of the Training dummies revamp is never alerted. SecondaryAttacks (sighsorry) already ships this exact detection, so ours stands down when it, GCO or SmartSkills is loaded. Standing still: a Player.UpdateStealth postfix adds a local SE_Stats with a negative m_stealthModifier (for example -0.5, which halves the stealth factor and so the view and alert ranges) after about 1 s crouched, grounded and not moving, and removes it on movement, standing up or leaving the ground. Stealth SEs add up and vanilla only clamps the sum to 0..1, so while ours is active the postfix floors the combined target (for example at 0.1, never above the value without our SE); stacked with the Sneak tiers of New ability depending on skill level it cannot reach 0. The SE is made at runtime with a unique name and added by reference, so it needs no ObjectDB entry; the vanilla stealth bar and the SE icon show the change. No XP for standing still (vanilla gives none, which keeps AFK farming out). With the Mob AI revamp, weak creatures ignore a player who outclasses them and usually stay unalerted: vanilla then pays the full 1 XP/s for crawling near them, and the first hit on each is a backstab whose XP the victim-HP scaling keeps near zero. Ships as Both: the server refuses players without the mod and sends its settings to everyone.
- **Hooks:** `Character.RPC_Damage`, `Player.RaiseSkill`, `Player.Awake`, `ZNetView.InvokeRPC`, `Character.GetFaction`, `Character.GetMaxHealth`, `Player.UpdateStealth`, `Character.IsSneaking`, `SEMan.AddStatusEffect`, `SEMan.RemoveStatusEffect`, `SEMan.ModifyStealth`, `Hud.UpdateStealth`
- **Risks:** The stealth factor moves at 0.25 per second, so stop-and-go crawling keeps part of the bonus: tune the delay or drop the bonus instantly on movement. Halving the view range makes ambushes and bow shots from cover much safer, so the numbers need server settings. Stacking: every SE_Stats stealth modifier adds base x modifier, so our SE plus the Sneak tiers of New ability depending on skill level (or other mods' stealth SEs) could reach a factor of 0, unseen at any range, without the floor; tune the floor with both loaded. Mob AI revamp: weak creatures near a strong player stay unalerted, so vanilla pays the full crawling XP (BaseAI.InStealthRange) and their first hit is a backstab; our XP has no floor on the victim-HP scaling, so they pay almost nothing. Training dummies: without the Faction.TrainingDummy exclusion, a row of dummies (never alerted when switched OFF by the Training dummies revamp) would pay backstab XP every 300 s each. Double XP: SecondaryAttacks already grants Sneak XP on every vanilla backstab with the same victim-owner detection, SmartSkills on hits against unaware enemies and GCO on its sneak bonus; detect them and skip ours, or document it. SecondaryAttacks recomputes the stealth target in its own Player.UpdateStealth postfix but still calls SEMan.ModifyStealth, so an SE modifier composes with it (writing m_stealthFactorTarget directly would be overwritten); SetUpSkills (transpiler on UpdateStealth), Sneaky Viking and ImpactfulSkills compose too. MC Harpoon Hooks Tames sets the backstab bonus to 1 on harpoon hits against tames, so hooking a tame never grants XP. Player.OnDeath clears all status effects, so the stillness check must re-add its SE.
- **Related:** Mob AI revamp; New ability depending on skill level; Training dummies revamp
- **Game systems:** [docs/game/combat.md](game/combat.md)
- **Existing mods:** partial

Also grants Sneak XP whenever any attack triggers the vanilla backstab (default 1 per backstab, server-synced): a Character.RPC_Damage prefix and postfix on the victim's owner compare m_backstabTime, and an RPC sends the XP to the attacker's client (source: BackstabSkillGainSystem.cs). By default it also doubles the skill-based part of the crouched visibility reduction and crouched speed at Sneak 100, and adds a knife Sneak Ambush that charges while crouched. Nothing depends on standing still. Tagged AI Generated and Deep North, about 12.7K downloads. Source: sighsorry1029/SecondaryAttacks. |
  | [SmartSkills (Smoothbrain)](https://thunderstore.io/c/valheim/p/Smoothbrain/SmartSkills/) | v1.0.2, updated 2024-06-21, 1.0: unknown (GitHub source got 1.0 fixes on 2026-09-12, not released) | Sneak raises backstab damage, and every hit on an unaware enemy (not alerted, no target) gives bonus Sneak XP (20 by default), decided on the attacker's client in a Character.Damage prefix with no crouch check and no cooldown (source). Open source: blaxxun-boop/SmartSkills. About 120K downloads. |

Nothing depends on movement. Source: NeocorDK/SetUpSkills. |

Its README names early detection as a problem but leaves the detection formula unchanged. Nexus 591. Reuploads (HHPatch, blacks7ar CombatOverhaulREwrite v1.2.5) are deprecated too. |

No stillness rule. Part of a large combat overhaul; tagged AI Generated, closed source, about 8.6K downloads. |

- **Inspiration:** The XP line already exists: SecondaryAttacks grants Sneak XP on every successful vanilla backstab, detected on the victim's owner through m_backstabTime with an RPC to the attacker (the same design as ours), GCO can award Sneak XP with its sneak bonus, and SmartSkills (pre-1.0 build) pays XP on any hit on an unaware enemy with no cooldown or crouch check. Kyresel's dead Combat Overhaul scaled the XP by the weapon's backstab bonus and gave less for projectiles. The two current ones ship inside large combat mods. Nobody rewards standing still: current mods scale sneak speed (SNEAKer), noise (Sneaky Viking, ImpactfulSkills) or the whole stealth curve (SetUpSkills, SecondaryAttacks). Ours: a small standalone, server-synced mod whose new part is the hold-still reward (the stealth factor halves after a second without moving, with a floor so stacked stealth effects never make the player invisible, shown by the vanilla stealth bar and a status icon), which makes ambushes a tactic without touching monster perception. The XP part borrows SecondaryAttacks' detection and Kyresel's lower XP for projectiles, is scaled by the victim's max HP with no floor (weak creatures pay almost nothing, training dummies nothing), and stands down when one of those mods already pays sneak XP.

### Trinket revamp

> Trinkets needing the adrenaline proc kinda sucks because the adrenaline system kinda suck. Tie this item to the Adrenaline revamp.  Proposal: keep trinket stats as is (bonus and adrenaline requirement), Make adrenaline generate passively during combat in addition to existing sources and not deplete over time. The trinket proc effect is an action the player needs to perform rather than an automatic proc. The player builds the adrenaline, and choses when to trigger the trinket.

- **Scope:** Revamp · **Feasibility:** easy · **Who needs it:** Client · **Custom assets:** no · **Status:** [Trinkets on Demand 0.1.0](../src/Combat/Trinkets.OnDemand) (in development)
- **Approach:** (1) Tied to the Adrenaline revamp: the build-up the sheet asks for (passive income while fighting, on top of the existing sources, and no decay over time) is that idea's point 2 and its no-decay rule (point 4), so this ships as a second feature of the same mod, or as its own mod with ModRequires on the adrenaline mod, which the framework turns off with a reason when the adrenaline mod is missing or disabled. (2) Trinket stats as they are: no SharedData edit; every trinket keeps its vanilla effect (m_fullAdrenalineSE, 30 to 120 s per the wiki) and cost (m_maxAdrenaline, 10 to 100), modded trinkets included. (3) No automatic pop, and a full bar stays full: after every call, Player.AddAdrenaline tests for a full bar, also when the call adds nothing, so in vanilla any gain at a full bar pops (it adds or refreshes every equipped m_fullAdrenalineSE, empties the bar and plays m_adrenalinePopEffects); with no equipped item that has one, vanilla sets the bar to the max and keeps it there. A Player.AddAdrenaline prefix (local player, feature active, outside our trigger) sets the m_fullAdrenalineSE of every equipped item to null for that call, and a finalizer puts the effects back, also after an exception. Vanilla then takes its own 'no effect' branch: no pop, and the bar is set to exactly the max, so later gains are ignored as vanilla does at the max. No transpiler is needed: the gain scaling (Game.m_adrenalineRate, the m_adrenalineGainMultiplier curve, SEMan.ModifyAdrenaline), the tier effects and other mods' patches run unchanged. It is safe because m_fullAdrenalineSE is read only by this method and the tooltip and nothing else runs during the call; only the outermost call hides and restores (a depth counter), because a status effect added during the call can call AddAdrenaline again (SE_Stats.StartupEffects). Other characters, and the local player while the feature is inactive, keep vanilla behaviour. Losses still count (the melee-miss and unblocked-hit penalties, off by default in the Adrenaline revamp); only the drain over time is gone. (4) Trigger: a Player.Update postfix for Player.m_localPlayer reads a configurable input. Keyboard default: a key vanilla leaves free, for example H (vanilla's default bindings put the Forsaken power GP on F, the radial menu on G, emotes on T, auto-pickup on V, sit on X, walk on C, hide on R, auto-run on Q and use on E, and debug mode reads Z, B, K and L in Player.Update). Gamepad, Default layout: the alt button (LT, which is also block) + right-stick click, which vanilla reads without the alt button for hide and the radial menu (Player.Update, Player.HandleRadialInput) and with it only in build mode; the D-pad is taken (down is the Forsaken power JoyGP, up and the sides the hotbar, alt + D-pad zooms the camera or the minimap). In the Alternative 1 and 2 layouts, alt + right-stick click is already the alternative-placement toggle (JoyAltPlace, which Player.Update reads outside build mode too), so those layouts need another default, to check in game. It works when Player.TakeInput is true (false while dead, in a menu or typing) and neither the radial menu (Hud.InRadial) nor placement mode (Player.InPlaceMode) is active, also mid-swing or mid-roll, because it plays no animation. With a full bar (GetAdrenaline() at least GetMaxAdrenaline(), max above 0) and at least one equipped item with an m_fullAdrenalineSE, it opens the trigger scope and calls Player.AddAdrenaline(0): the prefix leaves the effects in place and the vanilla pop runs unchanged (every equipped trinket fires, a running effect is refreshed, the bar empties, the pop effect plays). Cost: the full bar, as in vanilla. A partly filled bar does nothing except a short 'not full' message, like the Forsaken power's $hud_powernotready (Player.StartGuardianPower). A trigger while the effect still runs refreshes it and costs the full bar, as the vanilla pop does (SE_Stats.ResetTime restarts the time and re-runs the one-shot gains); an option can refuse the trigger instead while an equipped trinket's effect runs (SEMan.GetStatusEffect). (5) Feedback: when the bar becomes full, Hud.AdrenalineBarFlash and a message naming the key, with the flash repeated every few seconds while the bar stays full (config), plus an optional short vanilla sound (prefab picked at runtime, unverified). The trigger plays the vanilla pop effect (Player.m_adrenalinePopEffects; the SoftRef manifest_extended lists fx_Adrenaline1, unverified that it is this one). An ItemDrop.ItemData.GetTooltip postfix adds the key after the $item_fulladrenaline line. Hud.UpdateAdrenaline shows the bar whenever it is above 0, so a partly filled bar stays on screen between fights as a stored resource. (6) Edge cases: swapping trinkets keeps the bar; a bar above a cheaper trinket's cost counts as full, and the next adrenaline change sets it to the new max. With no trinket at all (max 0), vanilla drains and hides it (the no-decay rule only runs while the max is above 0). The bar is not saved (Player.Save does not write it, and nothing reads the ZDO adrenaline key), and death or logout spawns a new Player (Game.SpawnPlayer), so the bar starts empty; carrying it over through Player.m_customData is possible but not planned. Mods that add trinket slots fire every equipped trinket with one trigger, because the vanilla pop loops over all equipped items. Side: technically client-only (adrenaline, the pop and the key run on the wearer's client, the pop passes the effect object to SEMan.AddStatusEffect, and no item data changes for good, so a trinket reaching a player without the mod works as in vanilla); ships as Both (the server refuses players without the mod and sends its settings to everyone).
- **Hooks:** `Player.AddAdrenaline`, `Player.Update`, `Player.TakeInput`, `Hud.InRadial`, `Player.InPlaceMode`, `Player.GetAdrenaline`, `Player.GetMaxAdrenaline`, `SEMan.GetStatusEffect`, `Hud.AdrenalineBarFlash`, `Hud.UpdateAdrenaline`, `Player.Message`, `ItemDrop.ItemData.GetTooltip`
- **Risks:** Hiding m_fullAdrenalineSE changes shared item data for the length of one call: restore it in a finalizer, only in the outermost call. Another mod that reads the field inside Player.AddAdrenaline (its own prefix or postfix) sees no effect while it is hidden: cross-test MultiTrinket (no public source). Fallback if hiding proves fragile: a transpiler that adds one test to the full-bar branch, matched by its field and local loads, not by offsets, logging once and leaving the feature inactive with a reason when it does not match; KeepAdrenalineLonger also transpiles this method (the decay delay, a different instruction). Other Player.AddAdrenaline patches (the Adrenaline revamp, GrindstoneSkills, AdrenalineModifier) see the trigger as a call with 0 and pass it through; the Weapon revamp's parry detector only listens inside Humanoid.BlockAttack. If the player prefab lists tier effects in m_adrenalineEffects (AdrenalineRush to AdrenalineRush4 exist in the SoftRef manifest_extended; prefab data, unverified), a bar held at one level keeps that effect between fights: check at runtime. Balance: a bar that never drains means most fights start with a trigger ready, and it can be filled on training dummies (weapon hits on a dummy still pay) or weak mobs before a hard fight; tune with the Adrenaline revamp, and the server's settings apply to everyone. Keys: RageNAdrenaline defaults to F and G (and D-pad up and left), and SpecialAttack, WeaponArts and other mods add their own keys, so keep ours configurable, per gamepad layout too. Conflicts: BetterTrinkets and its 1.0 patch, Passive_Trinket_Modifiers and Balrond Battle Flow (its Surge holds a full bar, then its Overcharge drains it) change what trinkets give or when they fire, around the vanilla full-bar moment: detect them and step aside, or document it. MultiTrinket fires several trinkets together: cross-test. Surge only edits costs, and the bar follows the current max. The Blood trinket ignores a refresh (its ResetTime does nothing), so a trigger during its window spends the bar for nothing unless the refuse option is on. The NG+ world-level rule for equipping trinkets (Humanoid.EquipItem) still applies.
- **Related:** Adrenaline revamp; Blood trinket; Weapon revamp; Boss power revamp; Training dummies revamp; One trinket per trophy
- **Game systems:** [docs/game/combat.md](game/combat.md)
- **Existing mods:** partial

Tagged Deep North Update. No config yet, no source link. About 2.7K downloads. |

The linked repository has only a README. About 580 downloads. |

Its changelog adds an AdrenalineHoldover that counts the bar as full for a set time after a trinket fires, and a Tactician class that keeps 20% of the adrenaline used. No manual trigger. About 9.3K downloads. |

- **Inspiration:** Vanilla: a trinket adds adrenaline capacity (SharedData.m_maxAdrenaline), and its effect (m_fullAdrenalineSE, 30 to 120 s per the wiki) fires by itself the moment the bar is full (Player.AddAdrenaline), often at the end of a fight or while running away; the bar drains 6 to 10 s after the last gain, so the next fight starts from zero. The Forsaken power is the vanilla model of a power on a key, with a 'not ready' message. No mod found lets the player fire a vanilla trinket. RageNAdrenaline is the closest model (a meter that fills while enemies are near, a sound once it is full, spent with a key), but on meters of its own, with default keys that clash with vanilla actions (F is the Forsaken power, G the radial menu, D-pad up and left the hotbar). No decay exists only as a number: AdrenalineModifier's decay multiplier at 0 stops the drain but also removes the miss and unblocked-hit penalties and keeps the automatic pop, KeepAdrenalineLonger only lengthens the delay, and Odin (Nexus, per search snippets) stops the decay while enemies are near and adds per-trinket cooldowns. The passive-trinket mods (BetterTrinkets, Passive_Trinket_Modifiers, Balrond Battle Flow) no longer match: the sheet keeps the vanilla effects and costs, so they are conflicts. Borrow: RageNAdrenaline's key trigger and one-time 'full' sound, the Forsaken power's 'not ready' message, the vanilla bar flash, and MultiTrinket's 'fire every equipped trinket together', which the vanilla pop already does. Ours: vanilla trinket effects and costs with no lasting item data change, a bar that fills while fighting and never drains (the Adrenaline revamp), a full bar that waits with a flash, a message and a tooltip line naming the key, and one key (and a gamepad combination per layout) that fires every equipped trinket through the vanilla pop. Technically client-only; ships as Both, so the server refuses players without the mod and its settings apply to everyone.

### Adrenaline revamp

> Right now it is too long to build up adrenaline, specifically with a bow. It needs to be rebalanced properly across all combat systems. One easy first step would be to have a passiv income of adrenaline while in fight. Weapons adrenaline generation must also be reworked to reflect their active time (sword hits faster than bows). The goal should be to hit consistent trinket proc from combat to combat. Fighting one ennemy might not trigger it, but a large group should, Chaining combat from isolated mobs to isolated mobs should also trigger

- **Scope:** Revamp · **Feasibility:** medium · **Who needs it:** Client · **Custom assets:** no · **Status:** [Trinkets on Demand 0.1.0](../src/Combat/Trinkets.OnDemand) (in development)
- **Approach:** One part per sheet line. Technically client-only; ships as Both: the server refuses players without the mod and sends its settings to everyone. (1) Rebalance across all combat systems: a prefix on Player.AddAdrenaline rewrites the amount by source; it only changes the amount and never skips the call. The source comes from a [ThreadStatic] context set in prefixes (and cleared in finalizers) of the source methods: Attack.DoMeleeAttack, Attack.DoAreaAttack, Attack.OnAttackTrigger, Attack.FireProjectileBurst, Projectile.OnHit, Humanoid.BlockAttack, Character.RPC_Damage, Character.AddStaggerDamage, Character.RPC_AddAdrenaline (the stagger credit when another client owns the staggered creature), Player.RPC_HitWhileDodging, Player.ActivateGuardianPower and SE_Stats.StartupEffects. Block or parry: the Humanoid.BlockAttack prefix runs the vanilla parry test (m_timedBlockBonus above 1 and the private m_blockTimer between 0 and 0.25 s), the same test as the Weapon revamp's parry attack (one shared helper if both ship). Vanilla pays m_blockAdrenaline only when it is not a parry, and m_perfectBlockAdrenaline only when the parry held and there is an attacker, so an AddAdrenaline call inside that scope is a held parry when the test passed and a block otherwise. Because our prefix never skips the call, the Weapon revamp's detector (Priority.First or a postfix) still fires. A tower shield that cannot parry (Better tower shields) earns only the block amount. One config table covers defence (block, parry, perfect dodge, stagger), offence, kills, income and the two penalties (melee miss and unblocked hit, off by default). Character.m_enemyAdrenalineMultiplier is kept so swarm mobs stay cheap. Unknown sources, and weapon-hit amounts of 0 or less, pass through unchanged. (2) Passive income while in a fight: the local player counts as in combat for a few seconds after hitting an enemy, being hit by one, blocking, or being reported as the target of an alerted monster (Player.RPC_OnTargeted with alerted, the same signal that starts the combat music). That RPC carries no targeter, so it only counts while an alerted enemy that is not a dummy is near (BaseAI.GetAllInstances, IsAlerted, which any client reads from the ZDO). Faction.TrainingDummy is ignored for the combat state, the engaged count and the kill bonus: hitting a dummy is a player action and BaseAI.IsEnemy makes it an enemy, so a row of dummies would otherwise pay income, a large engaged count and full bars with no danger. Weapon hits on a dummy still pay (point 3), so a trinket can be tried out on one. While in combat, a Player.UpdateStats postfix adds base + perEnemy x max(0, engaged - 1) per second, capped, through AddAdrenaline once per second, so Game.m_adrenalineRate and status-effect modifiers still apply. Engaged = the sum of m_enemyAdrenalineMultiplier over the distinct enemies (by ZDOID) the player hit or was hit by in the last ~10 s, so a swarm of cheap mobs counts as one or two enemies. Income needs a player action in the last few seconds, so standing next to a stuck or caged monster earns nothing. (3) Weapon gains that follow active time: an attack that hits at least one enemy pays rate x attack time instead of a flat amount per hit, so every weapon earns the same per second of fighting. Attack time is learned per weapon (by m_dropPrefab name) from Attack.Start to Attack.Stop, plus the bow draw (the protected Humanoid.m_attackDrawTime, read at release in a Humanoid.StartAttack prefix) and the crossbow reload (ItemDrop.ItemData.GetWeaponLoadingTime). The Attack.Start hook is a postfix, so it sees the per-swing clone after the Weapon revamp's prefix has edited it. An attack whose m_attackAnimation differs from the weapon's own (the Attack it was cloned from: m_shared.m_attack for a primary attack, m_secondaryAttack for a secondary one, as the secondaryAttack argument of Humanoid.StartAttack says), such as a Weapon revamp roll, parry or jump attack (all primary attacks, also when one borrows the secondary animation), is not timed, because it would skew the learned time: it pays the weapon's learned amount x the clone's m_attackAdrenaline / the weapon's own m_attack.m_attackAdrenaline, so the Weapon revamp's per-move bonus survives. These moves cost no adrenaline. A per-skill config table seeds the time until it is measured. Melee pays once per swing, plus a small capped bonus per extra enemy hit. Projectiles pay per hit, divided by the projectiles per attack. Staff m_attackUseAdrenaline follows the same rule. (4) A full bar every fight, also across chained fights: a kill bonus from Game.RPC_RegisterKill (it runs on every credited player's client), larger for bosses (bossNumber > 0). The RPC only carries the creature's name token, boss number, kill modifiers and attacker count, so the bonus is paid only when the name matches an enemy the player fought in this combat (a dummy never enters that list), scaled by that enemy's m_enemyAdrenalineMultiplier. No decay: while a trinket is equipped (max above 0), a Player.UpdateStats(float) prefix raises the private m_adrenalineDegenTimer to at least 1 s before vanilla counts it down, so the vanilla drain never runs and the bar keeps what it earned between fights; lone fights chain on their own. With no trinket, vanilla drains the bar as before. With the Trinket revamp, a full bar then waits for the player's key; without it, vanilla pops the trinket as soon as the bar fills. Starting targets, to tune: one enemy fills about half of a 60-cost trinket; a group of four or five, or two or three chained lone fights, fill it. The Trinket revamp keeps the vanilla trinket costs (10 to 100), so tune against them. Small extra: a debug readout of gains per source per fight. The bar only feeds the trinket, which the player triggers (Trinket revamp): Weapon revamp moves cost no adrenaline, and boss powers keep their own cooldown (activation still gives +10).
- **Hooks:** `Player.AddAdrenaline`, `Player.UpdateStats`, `Player.RPC_OnTargeted`, `Player.IsTargeted`, `Character.Damage`, `Character.RPC_Damage`, `Character.RPC_AddAdrenaline`, `BaseAI.IsEnemy`, `BaseAI.IsAlerted`, `BaseAI.HaveTarget`, `BaseAI.GetAllInstances`, `Character.GetFaction`, `Attack.DoMeleeAttack`, `Attack.DoAreaAttack`, `Attack.OnAttackTrigger`, `Attack.FireProjectileBurst`, `Projectile.OnHit`, `Humanoid.BlockAttack`, `Character.AddStaggerDamage`, `Player.RPC_HitWhileDodging`, `Player.ActivateGuardianPower`, `SE_Stats.StartupEffects`, `Humanoid.StartAttack`, `Attack.Start`, `Attack.Stop`, `Humanoid.GetAttackDrawPercentage`, `ItemDrop.ItemData.GetWeaponLoadingTime`, `Game.RPC_RegisterKill`, `Hud.UpdateAdrenaline`
- **Risks:** The curves m_adrenalineDegen, m_adrenalineDegenDelay and m_adrenalineGainMultiplier, each weapon's m_attackAdrenaline and m_attackUseAdrenaline, each projectile's m_adrenaline and each creature's m_enemyAdrenalineMultiplier are prefab data, so dump them before choosing numbers. The wiki values differ from the code defaults (perfect dodge 5 vs 10, stagger 3 vs 5). Without a trinket the max is 0 and AddAdrenaline ignores gains, so nothing changes. Farming: training dummies are excluded by faction (point 2), but a trapped mob is a real enemy, so require recent damage dealt or taken and cap income per fight. With no decay, a bar filled anywhere is kept, so weapon hits on a dummy (or on weak mobs) can fill it before a hard fight: keep the vanilla dummy pay (to try a trinket out) or pay nothing for dummy hits, a decision to tune. Context tagging misses AddAdrenaline calls from other mods, which pass through unchanged. Game.m_adrenalineRate and SE modifiers (for example GP_Fader's +100%, per the wiki and BossRules' table of the vanilla effects; prefab value, unverified) still multiply every gain, income included. Other mods change the same gains: KeepAdrenalineLonger (transpiler on Player.AddAdrenaline, flat 15 s decay delay), AdrenalineModifier (prefix on it that scales gains and decay, postfix that scales the decay timer), GrindstoneSkills (prefix on it that scales block, parry and unblocked-hit amounts), and Balrond Battle Flow and Passive_Trinket_Modifiers (global gain multiplier), which have no source link. Give our prefix an explicit Harmony priority; it never skips the call, so the Weapon revamp's parry detector still sees it. The MC mod Harpoon Hooks Tames sets Projectile.m_adrenaline to 0 for a hooked tame, and that 0 must stay 0. Adrenaline is not saved. The tuning depends on each trinket's cost. Ships as Both, so the server's settings apply to everyone.
- **Related:** Trinket revamp; Blood trinket; Boss power revamp; Weapon revamp; Better tower shields; Training dummies revamp; Dual wielding
- **Game systems:** [docs/game/combat.md](game/combat.md)

Weapon-type bonuses grow with the bar, trinkets scale from 0% power at 0 adrenaline to 100% at 100 adrenaline, a Surge holds the bar at max, then an Overcharge drains it with bonuses and drawbacks. - **Inspiration:** Vanilla: with no trinket the player's own max is 0 (Surge's README, code default 100). The wiki gives +1 per hit for one-handed weapons and +2 for two-handed weapons, polearms, bows and crossbows, parry +5, perfect dodge +5, block +1 or +2, stagger +3 and Forsaken power +10. The bar drains after 6 to 10 s without a gain, at 1 to 4 per second. Melee pays per character hit in a swing, an arrow pays once, and a bow's draw only gets shorter with skill, which is why bows fall behind. A Steam thread ('trinkets need a rework', 2025-09) says the build-up is too slow and asks for passives. A player's reply there explains that the decay delay shrinks from 10 to 6 s as the bar fills, and that an AoE weapon that staggers several mobs earns several chunks per hit. Borrow: KeepAdrenalineLonger's longer hold and AdrenalineModifier's decay multiplier, taken to the end (no decay while a trinket is equipped), RageNAdrenaline's 'fills while enemies are near' and TastyAdrenaline's per-tick trickle as income, and Battle Flow's framing of adrenaline as combat momentum. What they get wrong: the number mods scale every source alike, so bows stay behind swords. Battle Flow changes what the bar does, not how fast it fills. RageNAdrenaline adds a second meter instead of fixing the vanilla one, and TastyAdrenaline's income depends on a mead, not on fighting. Ours: income while fighting that grows with the number of enemies engaged, weapon gains per second of attacking, a kill bonus, and no decay, so chained lone fights add up. It is tuned so the bar of a mid-cost trinket fills about once per real fight, and the player then triggers the trinket (Trinket revamp). Technically client-only; ships as Both, so the server refuses players without the mod and its settings apply to everyone.

### Balista revamp

> Balista are slow to turn and shoot, which makes them almost useless against most enemies that move above the speed of a snail. The goal is to make them smarter at acquire target (better aim, turns faster), and make them able to have multiple targets assignement

- **Scope:** Revamp · **Feasibility:** medium · **Who needs it:** Both · **Custom assets:** no · **Status:** idea
- **Approach:** Turns faster: config multipliers on m_turnRate (45 degrees per second in the prefab dump), m_lookAcceleration (1.2) and m_lookDeacceleration (0.05), set per instance in a Turret.Awake postfix and re-applied or restored on a live config change; the deceleration term is what makes the last 11 degrees slow, because there the speed falls in proportion to the remaining angle. Shoots more: keep tracking during the 2 s reload (Turret.IsCoolingDown answers false while UpdateTurretRotation runs; vanilla freezes the turret), replace the fixed 1.6 degree fire window (m_shootWhenAimDiff 0.9999 as a quaternion dot) with 'the predicted miss at the target is smaller than its radius', and offer a multiplier on m_attackCooldown (default: vanilla). Better aim: a prefix on UpdateTurretRotation replaces the has-a-target branch with a 3D intercept (vanilla takes the flight time from Vector2.Distance, which reads only x and y, then doubles the lead with m_predictionModifier 2), iterated two or three times, aimed at Character.GetCenterPoint and computed from m_eye (vanilla aims from m_turretBody's pivot while the bolt leaves from m_eye); the clamp to the arc and the Utils.RotateTorwardsSmooth call are inline code, not a method to call, so the prefix repeats them (about 25 lines) or a small transpiler replaces only the flight-time term. Turret projectiles have m_gravity 0 and m_drag 0, so bolts fly straight at constant speed and need no drop compensation; optional spread reduction by scaling the ammo's m_projectileAccuracy inside ShootProjectile. Smarter target choice: prefix-replace UpdateTarget with a score over the creatures FindClosestCreature's rules accept: keep the current target while it lives and stays in the arc and in sight (vanilla re-picks the closest every second), search again at once after a kill, prefer a small turn from the current aim, a low angular speed, a clear line of fire from m_eye and threats (BaseAI.HaveTarget or IsAlerted, both ZDO data readable by any owner), and skip m_aiSkipTarget characters such as the T.W.I.G.; keep RPC_SetTarget to everybody. Several targets: raise m_maxConfigTargets (config); vanilla code already stores, reads, matches and lists any number of trophies and drops the oldest when full; optional BottleShips-style mode where trophy kinds come first and other hostiles after. Small extra, not asked: neighbouring ballistas spread their fire using the RPC_SetTarget-synced targets of other turrets. Friendly fire is out of scope (existing mods handle it).
- **Hooks:** `Turret.Awake`, `Turret.UpdateTurretRotation`, `Turret.IsCoolingDown`, `Turret.UpdateTarget`, `Turret.UpdateAttack`, `Turret.ShootProjectile`, `Turret.RPC_SetTarget`, `Turret.UseItem`, `Turret.SetTargets`, `Turret.ReadTargets`, `Turret.GetHoverText`, `BaseAI.FindClosestCreature`, `BaseAI.CanSeeTarget`, `BaseAI.HaveTarget`, `BaseAI.IsAlerted`, `Utils.RotateTorwardsSmooth`, `Character.GetCenterPoint`, `Character.GetVelocity`
- **Risks:** Target choice, the fire decision and the shot direction run on the turret's ZDO owner, any nearby client, so every player needs the mod (house rule: the server refuses players without it); a ballista owned by a client without it behaves like vanilla. The Ashlands enemy piece_Charred_Balista and the trap fuling_turret use the same Turret component: change only piece_turret by default. Prefab values come from a third-party 1.0 dump (ServersideQoL docs) and the aim-lag figures are computed from the code, not measured: dump piece_turret at runtime first. ReBallista, BetterBallistas, Turrets Redo, TurretRevamped, ValheimPlus, ZenWorldSettings and BottleShips change the same methods or fields: detect them and turn the overlapping parts off (ValheimFortress's Automated Ballista has its own component and does not conflict). The aim fix either repeats UpdateTurretRotation's inline clamp and rotation code or uses a transpiler: re-check it after every game update. UpdateTarget and UpdateTurretRotation run in FixedUpdate: keep scoring allocation-free and throttled. A ballista that rarely misses is strong base defence: keep the reload and the 40-bolt magazine.
- **Related:** Mob AI revamp; Training dummies revamp

  | [ServersideQoL SmartDefense (ArgusMagnus)](https://thunderstore.io/c/valheim/p/ArgusMagnus/ServersideQoL_SmartDefense/) | v2.1.0, updated 2026-09-25, 1.0: likely | Server-side only: ballistas do not target players or tames and reload from nearby containers. Friendly fire only. |
  | [ImFRIENDLY DAMMIT (Azumatt)](https://thunderstore.io/c/valheim/p/Azumatt/ImFRIENDLY_DAMMIT/) | v1.1.9, updated 2025-03-14, 1.0: dead (deprecated) | Ballistas never hit you or your tames. Friendly fire only. |
  | [G3A3 (G3A1)](https://thunderstore.io/c/valheim/p/G3A1/G3A3/) | v1.9.75, updated 2026-09-29, 1.0: likely | Large gameplay pack whose Ballista section lists a 'smart ballista' and ballista auto-refill; the README gives no details. |

- **Inspiration:** Why vanilla ballistas miss (code read with ServersideQoL's 1.0 dump of piece_turret): the last degrees of turning slow down in proportion to the remaining angle while the ballista only fires within about 1.6 degrees, so a creature whose bearing changes faster than about 6 degrees per second is followed but never shot; the turret freezes for its 2 s reload; the lead uses only the x and y distance and is doubled; the aim is taken from the body's pivot while the bolt leaves from the eye; the closest creature is re-picked every second. Borrow ReBallista's lower aim for short creatures and optional spread removal, BottleShips' 'trophy kinds first, other hostiles after' as an option, trophy icons on hover (ZenHoverItem) once a ballista holds several trophies, BetterBallistas' finding that only m_maxConfigTargets = 1 blocks several trophies, and the target rules of ValheimFortress's own Automated Ballista (keep the target until it dies, pick the closest enemy with a clear line) and of Zarkow Turret Defense (threat levels). Nobody applies such rules to the vanilla ballista or keeps it tracking during the reload; ReBallista's homing bolts bend the physics and MultiTargetBallista replaces the vanilla trophy flow. Ours: vanilla-looking ballistas that turn visibly faster, lead correctly, keep a target until it dies and take several trophies through the vanilla hotbar flow.

### Better tower shields

> Make them usable. I see them as a immovable wall that can't parry: The player should be slowed A LOT but should be able to block virtually anything idea, make them TWO HANDED, very slow, with a small shield bash attack that has heavy stagger numbers but low damage number, and a lot of block armor

- **Scope:** Revamp · **Feasibility:** medium · **Who needs it:** Client · **Custom assets:** no · **Status:** [Tower Shield Wall 0.1.0](../src/Combat/Shields.TowerWall) (in development)
- **Approach:** Pick tower shields from a configurable prefab list: ShieldWoodTower, ShieldBoneTower, ShieldIronTower, ShieldSerpentscale (the wiki lists the Serpent Scale Shield with the tower shields), ShieldBlackmetalTower, ShieldFlametalTower and ShieldGoldTower. FW_ShieldBlackmetalTower and SP_ShieldBlackmetalTower are separate prefabs (their prefixes match the FallenWarrior and ShadowPerson creature prefabs, unverified) and stay untouched. An ObjectDB.Awake/CopyOtherDB postfix edits their SharedData. (1) Set m_itemType to TwoHandedWeaponLeft and m_attachOverride to Shield. Vanilla Humanoid.EquipItem then empties both hands when the shield is equipped, and a one-handed weapon or torch replaces it. Humanoid.Pickup stops auto-equipping weapons while it is held, and a picked-up tower shield now counts as a weapon (IsWeapon), so it is auto-equipped when the right hand is empty. The back model and the armor stand slot follow m_attachOverride, so they stay those of a shield. The tooltip says two-handed and shows damage. (2) Keep m_timedBlockBonus at 1 or lower so it cannot parry (the wiki already lists no parry bonus for tower shields, so this guards against other mods), and set m_perfectBlockAdrenaline to 0, because the tooltip shows a parry-adrenaline line whenever it is above 0. (3) Multiply m_blockPower and m_blockPowerPerLevel (the block armor). In the vanilla block formula, block armor far above the hit's damage cuts the damage taken, the stamina per block, the stagger and the knockback. (4) Make m_movementModifier more negative, but keep it above -0.67. (5) Set m_attack to a clone of the player's unarmed combo (Humanoid.m_unarmedWeapon), which already plays in the shield stance. No player exists yet when ObjectDB loads, so take it from the player prefab (Game.m_playerPrefab) or clone it on first use, as CaptainValheim does at attack time. Make it a low-damage, heavy-stagger bash: small flat blunt m_damages per tier (not derived from block armor) and a large m_staggerMultiplier on the cloned Attack (for example 5 to 10, to tune). Character.ApplyDamage adds the landed blunt, slash, pierce and lightning damage (after resistances and armor) times HitData.m_staggerMultiplier to the victim's stagger bar, Attack.DoMeleeAttack copies the multiplier from the Attack, and HitData serializes it, so a vanilla victim owner applies it. Keep the multiplier under 100, because Character.RPC_Damage staggers anything hit with 100 or more at once, before the dodge and block checks. Add a moderate m_attackForce, a short range and a low stamina cost, m_skillType Blocking (the random skill factor then scales damage and stagger with the Blocking skill, and the bash trains Blocking), and m_blockable/m_dodgeable true. m_skillType Blocking also keeps the Weapon revamp off the bash: that idea leaves attacks with skill Blocking vanilla, so its roll and jump attacks never replace the bash or stack their x2 stagger on its multiplier. A staggered non-player takes double damage from the next hits and the bearer gains stagger adrenaline, so the bash is crowd control that sets up allies. Humanoid.GetCurrentWeapon then returns the shield, so the normal attack button bashes through vanilla StartAttack, and GetCurrentBlocker still blocks with it. The shield has no secondary attack unless m_secondaryAttack is set too (today a vanilla shield with an empty right hand uses the unarmed secondary, a kick per mod READMEs, unverified). While blocking, an UpdateBlock postfix adds a local SE_Stats brace slow, which also slows walking, crouching and turning. An RPC_Damage prefix makes frontal hits blockable, filtered by HitType (fall hits use the ground normal as direction and pass the facing test). A Character.AddStaggerDamage prefix, scoped to BlockAttack, skips or scales the stagger while stamina remains, because a stagger ends IsBlocking and the next hits land unblocked. The damage that still lands adds stagger again in Character.ApplyDamage, times the hit's multiplier; if that still breaks the wall, keep the scope open for the rest of that RPC_Damage call (prefix plus finalizer). Rear hits and stamina stay the counterplay. Everything runs on the bearer's own client: equipping, attacks and movement run there, the bash's HitData is built by the attacker, and incoming hits are resolved by the victim's owner. So it is technically client-only. It changes combat balance, so it ships as Both: the server refuses players without the mod and sends its settings to everyone.
- **Hooks:** `ObjectDB.Awake`, `ObjectDB.CopyOtherDB`, `Character.RPC_Damage`, `Humanoid.BlockAttack`, `Character.AddStaggerDamage`, `Humanoid.UpdateBlock`, `Character.ApplyPushback`, `Aoe.OnHit`, `ItemDrop.ItemData.GetTooltip`, `Player.GetJogSpeedFactor`, `Humanoid.EquipItem`, `Humanoid.GetCurrentWeapon`, `Humanoid.StartAttack`
- **Risks:** Changing m_itemType is global and breaks code that looks for ItemType.Shield. CaptainValheim's shield techniques check it (seen in its source). EpicLoot's shield enchant rules and auto-equip-shield features such as ZenCombat's or ShieldMeBruh's (about 116K downloads) probably do too (unverified). MC's own shared ItemKinds classifier (Sort Chest, Crafting Search and Sort) would file tower shields under weapons unless it also reads m_attachOverride. Because a tower shield now counts as a weapon, Humanoid.Pickup auto-equips a picked-up one when the right hand is empty, replacing the shield in hand. ItemStand.CanAttach matches its supported types against m_itemType, not m_attachOverride, so an item stand that lists Shield but not TwoHandedWeaponLeft would refuse tower shields (stand lists unverified). Fallback: keep ItemType.Shield, enforce two hands with Humanoid.EquipItem postfixes, and swap in the bash through a GetCurrentWeapon/StartAttack override, as CaptainValheim does. EquipItem enforces hands only at equip time, so turning the feature on while a sword and a tower shield are held must unequip the sword. Vanilla block charges fire the shield's own m_attack through Attack.StartWithoutAnimation. A mod that turns m_buildBlockCharges on (ZenCombat, CaptainValheim) would fire our bash as that counter. The heavy stagger only comes from hits that land: a blocking target takes stagger in Humanoid.BlockAttack from the damage left after block armor, without the hit's multiplier; Character.ApplyDamage returns before the stagger step when the hit deals 0.1 or less after resistances, armor and Game.m_playerDamageRate; blunt-resistant targets take less; creatures with m_staggerDamageFactor 0 or less never stagger from damage (which ones is prefab data); and if the cloned unarmed combo has several chain levels (prefab data), its last hit doubles damage and so stagger. The equipment movement modifier only scales jog and run speed, run, jump and dodge stamina, and knockback. Walk and crouch speed ignore it, so with the code defaults (jog 10, walk 5) a modifier below -0.5 makes walking faster than jogging. The run factor reaches 0 at -0.67 and the jog factor at -1. Below that, Character.UpdateWalking gets a negative speed that nothing clamps unless an SE_Stats is active. The rest of the slow therefore needs an SE. An SE speed of -1 or lower roots the player and turns jumps into weak tired jumps. Some damage breaks the block or never reaches BlockAttack. Hits with m_staggerMultiplier of 100 or more stagger before the block check. Aoe.m_blockable is false in code by default, and fire (CinderFire) hits are unblockable. AoE launches (ForceJump) and Aoe.m_knockBackForce pushes are applied in Aoe.OnHit, which a networked AoE runs on its owner's machine, so the bearer's client may not be the one applying them. DoTs, lava, falls and drowning skip blocking. Stagger is counted twice: BlockAttack adds it from the damage left after block armor, and ApplyDamage adds it again from what lands, times m_staggerMultiplier. Shield base values, block-charge settings and the vanilla slow are prefab data (unverified): the wiki says -10% for tower shields, Make Tower Shields Great Again's page says -20%. CaptainValheim (BlockAttack prefix and transpiler, GetCurrentWeapon, StartAttack, Pickup, RPC_Damage), ReliableBlock and ReliableBlockRebuilt (BlockAttack transpilers), Combat Adjustments (Character.GetStaggerTreshold postfix), GCO and ZenCombat (closed source) and EpicLoot (GetBlockPower, unverified) touch the same code. The MC mods Crossbow Stays Loaded (BlockAttack postfix, UnequipItem prefix, GetTooltip postfix), Harpoon Hooks Tames (RPC_Damage prefix and postfix) and Forge Idol Upgrades (ObjectDB.Awake/CopyOtherDB and GetTooltip patches, idols only) need cross tests. Huge block armor plus a staggering bash is a PvP balance issue; the server settings (the mod ships as Both) keep the values equal for everyone. Every stagger the bash causes pays the bearer m_staggerEnemyAdrenaline x the victim's m_enemyAdrenalineMultiplier (Character.AddStaggerDamage), so a cheap bash that staggers often is a steady adrenaline source: tune it with the Adrenaline revamp, which rewrites the stagger amount. RPC_Damage is hot and long, so patch it with a prefix (plus a finalizer to close scopes) only, no transpiler.
- **Related:** Weapon revamp; Adrenaline revamp; Dual wielding
- **Game systems:** [docs/game/combat.md](game/combat.md)
- **Existing mods:** partial

Tower shields hold until a blocked hit empties your stamina. Successful blocks prevent stagger and knockback, cover a wider angle and reduce the remaining physical damage. Surplus damage drains stamina instead of health, and no hit staggers you while stamina remains after the block. 40% less block stamina than other shields (Nexus snippet), halved knockback on a successful block, and 'stickiness' to the opponent. - **Inspiration:** Vanilla already provides most of the parts: the TwoHandedWeaponLeft equip rules, the unarmed combo (it already plays while a shield is held), a block formula where very high block armor absorbs almost everything (less damage, stamina, stagger and push per hit), a block-charge counter that is switched off, and a stagger lever independent of damage: a landed hit adds its physical and lightning damage times HitData.m_staggerMultiplier to the victim's stagger bar, so 'low damage, heavy stagger' is a small blunt value with a large multiplier on the bash's Attack. Borrow: from GCO, 'hold until stamina is empty, no stagger or knockback'; from ReliableBlock (and its 1.0 fork) and ZenCombat, the block still counts when a hit staggers you (ours goes further and prevents that stagger while stamina lasts); from CaptainValheim, the empty-hand strike built from the cloned unarmed attack with Blocking skill scaling; from Make Tower Shields Great Again, knockback on block; from GrindstoneSkills, the optional idea of a block that staggers the attacker. What they get wrong: GCO locks the stance inside a huge overhaul. CaptainValheim and ShieldBash give every shield a strike whose damage grows with block power and keep tower shields one-handed, so you still carry a sword. Make Tower Shields Great Again only changes numbers and depends on mods from before 1.0, and Combat Adjustments only enlarges the stagger bar. Ours gives tower shields their own identity: two-handed under the vanilla rules, very slow when carried and slower still while bracing, much more block armor, no parry, a frontal block that also stops AoE and projectiles, and a small, cheap bash on the normal attack button with low damage and heavy stagger, a crowd-control tool that sets up allies. Detect GCO, ZenCombat, CaptainValheim, ShieldBash and ReliableBlock (either version) and warn.

### Blood magic XP

> Gain XP when sacrificing HP, and when any shield is HIT (not only on broken) It should also provide blood magic skill XP to the caster of the shield, not the wearer of the shield

- **Scope:** Revamp · **Feasibility:** medium · **Who needs it:** Both · **Custom assets:** no · **Status:** idea
- **Approach:** Wrap Character.UseHealth with a prefix and postfix for the local player while Humanoid.m_currentAttack uses a Blood Magic weapon. Raise Blood Magic by the share of max HP actually lost times a config rate. Vanilla only calls UseHealth from Attack.Update and Attack.FireProjectileBurst, and paying HP gives no XP. Postfix StatusEffect.SetAttacker on SE_Shield instances to remember the caster: Character.RPC_Damage passes the caster after it adds or refreshes the bubble, and vanilla throws it away. Around SE_Shield.OnDamaged, measure what was really absorbed (the change in m_damage, capped at the remaining capacity) and keep only hits from an attacker other than the wearer. Turn that into XP in proportion to the share of the bubble's capacity used. On break (SE_Shield.IsDone prefix), give the vanilla m_levelUpSkillFactor to the caster instead of the wearer. A local caster gets the XP through Player.RaiseSkill. A remote caster gets it through a routed RPC on their Player ZNetView, sent only when their ZDO says they have the mod; otherwise vanilla rules apply and the wearer keeps the break XP. Optionally, route summon XP the same way.
- **Hooks:** `Character.UseHealth`, `Attack.Update`, `Attack.FireProjectileBurst`, `Attack.GetAttackHealth`, `Humanoid.m_currentAttack`, `Player.RaiseSkill`, `SE_Shield.OnDamaged`, `SE_Shield.IsDone`, `SE_Shield.SetLevel`, `StatusEffect.SetAttacker`, `Character.RPC_Damage`, `Player.Awake`, `ZNetView.InvokeRPC`, `Character.RaiseSkill`
- **Risks:** Besides attacker hits, the bubble absorbs every attacker-less Character.Damage hit: fall (Character.UpdateGroundContact), drowning (Player.OnSwimming), Ashlands lava and hot ocean (Character.UpdateHeatDamage), the edge of the world, and SE_Wet and SE_Stats damage ticks. A character's own AoE (Aoe.m_hitOwner) hits it with itself as the attacker. HitData.GetTotalDamage also counts chop and pickaxe damage and, in NG+, the world-level enemy bonus. So only hits from other attackers may count, with a cap per hit, per bubble and per minute. Absorb scaling with skill is unverified: the wiki says 200 + 5 per level, but the skill only reaches SetLevel when the shield Aoe keeps m_useAttackSettings, and SkeletonCrew's README says vanilla never scales it. Only eitr stops a cast-heal-recast loop. Mods that turn eitr costs into HP costs remove that limit, and recast-refill mods (BubbleBar, ShieldOverflowFix, Better Staff Of Protection) let one bubble absorb without end. The caster reference exists only in the status effect on the wearer's current owner, so a logout or respawn loses the credit, and an ownership change drops the bubble itself. Both sides need the mod: an RPC to an unmodded caster is simply lost (a ZDO-targeted one only logs 'Failed to find rpc method'), which is why the ZDO capability flag is needed to fall back to vanilla. ImpactfulSkills, SmartSkills, BloodMagicCompanion, EitrMagicExtended, SecondaryAttacks and BubbleBar patch the same methods, so detect them to avoid paying XP twice. Prefab values (the Staff_shield skill and XP factor, the shield Aoe settings, staff HP and eitr costs) must be dumped at runtime first.
- **Related:** New magical items based on valheim magic (ward, heal); New summons; Magic applied to non combat (craft, farming, cooking, sailing, etc,); Blood trinket
- **Game systems:** [docs/game/combat.md](game/combat.md)
