An optional Staff of Protection secondary attack removes your own shield without XP; it copies the primary attack at half the eitr and half the HP-percentage cost, so an HP-sacrifice XP rule would pay for it. Open source (Unlicense, shudnal/EitrMagicExtended). About 21.6K downloads. |
  | [BubbleBar (pandincus)](https://thunderstore.io/c/valheim/p/pandincus/BubbleBar/) | v0.1.0, 2026-09-15, tagged AI Generated (1.0: likely) | No XP change, but a compatibility trap. Shows the barrier's remaining capacity and, by default (RestoreOnRefresh), refills the barrier on recast through an SE_Shield.SetLevel postfix that sets m_damage to 0, on the client that receives the barrier. With XP per share of capacity used, refills let one bubble pay without end. Open source (pandincus/valheim-mods). 128 downloads. |
  | [ShieldOverflowFix (NxDimi)](https://thunderstore.io/c/valheim/p/NxDimi/ShieldOverflowFix/) | v1.0.0, 2026-03-15, tagged AI Generated (1.0: unknown) | No XP change. Lets damage beyond the barrier's remaining capacity through instead of blocking the whole hit, and can refill the barrier on recast (option). Changes the numbers an absorbed-damage XP rule measures. No source link. 235 downloads. |

- **Inspiration:** Vanilla (1.0.16): paying HP for a staff gives no XP. SE_Shield.IsDone gives m_levelUpSkillFactor XP only when damage breaks the bubble, never on expiry. That XP goes to the wearer through Skills.RaiseSkill, so a shielded tame gives nothing (Character.GetSkills returns null for non-players). The caster passed to StatusEffect.SetAttacker is thrown away. Borrow SecondaryAttacks' before/after wrapper on UseHealth: it pays for HP actually lost and respects the 1 HP floor. Borrow ImpactfulSkills' XP for absorbed damage, and BloodMagicCompanion's capability flag with a fallback to vanilla. What existing mods get wrong: ImpactfulSkills pays whoever simulates the wearer and counts fall, drowning, chop and pickaxe damage. SmartSkills records the wrong caster for shields on other players, and its expiry option (on by default) removes the break XP. BloodMagicCompanion only moves the break reward. SecondaryAttacks pays per raw HP, so more food HP levels you faster. Ours is one small mod with three rules. HP sacrifice XP scales with the share of max HP paid. Shield XP scales with the share of the bubble's capacity that other attackers' hits used up, with a cap per bubble so recast-refill mods (BubbleBar, ShieldOverflowFix) cannot farm it. All shield XP (hits and break) goes to the caster through a routed RPC, falling back to vanilla when either side lacks the mod. Optionally, it also fixes summon XP that is lost when another client simulates the summon.

### Mob AI revamp

> Being fully geared from Ashlands and still being attacked by neck and boars does not make sense. Weak enemies should not try to attack the player (except if frighten). Groups of enemies should enter a "flee" mode if their leader is taken down (for example, Greydwarfs would run away if a troll, shaman, or greydwarf brute is killed)

- **Scope:** Revamp · **Feasibility:** medium · **Who needs it:** Both · **Custom assets:** no · **Status:** [Creature Morale 0.1.0](../src/Combat/Creatures.Morale) (in development)
- **Approach:** Player strength: each player's client writes its real armor (Player.GetBodyArmor, status-effect armor included) to a custom float in its player ZDO about once per second; max HP is already in ZDO max_health. A creature is weak against a player when its best attack (highest total damage without chop and pickaxe among its inventory items aimed at enemies, times the star factor 1 + 0.5 x (level - 1), the player-count scale and Game.m_enemyDamageRate, plus the NG+ flat bonus that HitData.GetTotalDamage adds for creature attackers) does less than a config share (for example 5%) of the player's max HP after HitData.DamageTypes.ApplyArmor; cache per prefab and level. Do not attack: a BaseAI.FindEnemy postfix on MonsterAI owners drops an outclassing player unless the creature is provoked, and a MonsterAI.UpdateAI prefix drops such a target and, while the creature senses that player (BaseAI.CanSenseTarget), calmly walks away (Flee while not alerted) when the player comes within a keep-away range; a crouched player it cannot sense can still close in. Except if frightened: a MonsterAI.OnDamaged postfix stores provoked-until in the creature ZDO when a player hits it, so it fights back for a while (a hit from a tame or a dummy only makes it fight that attacker, as in vanilla); a cornered creature (player within 3 m for 3 s) fights back too. Bosses, tames, raid creatures and training dummies (Faction.TrainingDummy: they run MonsterAI, so the weak check would stop them training players) are never affected. Side effect on Sneak: weak creatures usually stay unalerted near a player who outclasses them, so vanilla Player.OnSneaking pays the full 1 XP/s for crawling near them (BaseAI.InStealthRange) and the first hit on each is a backstab (the Sneak revamp's victim-HP scaling keeps its XP near zero). Leader down: a config table maps leaders to followers (Troll, Greydwarf_Shaman and Greydwarf_Elite, the Greydwarf Brute, lead Greydwarf and Greyling; optional Goblin, Draugr and Seeker pairs). A BaseAI.OnDeath postfix on the leader's owner finds followers within a radius in BaseAI.BaseAIInstances (prefab hash from ZDO.GetPrefab) and invokes a rout RPC on each follower's ZNetView, which reaches its owner. The owner stores rout-until in the follower ZDO; while it lasts the MonsterAI.UpdateAI prefix runs the base update (reverse patch of BaseAI.UpdateAI), clears the target, keeps the creature alerted so it runs rather than walks, flees from the death point and returns false; afterwards the creature calms down (not alerted, no target). Tames and, by default, raid creatures are not routed. Ships as Both: the server refuses players without the mod and sends its settings to everyone.
- **Hooks:** `MonsterAI.UpdateAI`, `BaseAI.UpdateAI`, `BaseAI.FindEnemy`, `BaseAI.CanSenseTarget`, `BaseAI.Flee`, `BaseAI.SetAlerted`, `MonsterAI.OnDamaged`, `BaseAI.OnDeath`, `MonsterAI.Awake`, `ZNetView.InvokeRPC`, `BaseAI.BaseAIInstances`, `ZDO.GetPrefab`, `Player.GetBodyArmor`, `Character.GetMaxHealth`, `Humanoid.GetInventory`, `ItemDrop.ItemData.GetDamage`, `HitData.DamageTypes.ApplyArmor`, `Game.GetDifficultyDamageScalePlayer`, `MonsterAI.IsEventCreature`, `MonsterAI.HuntPlayer`, `Character.IsBoss`, `Character.IsTamed`, `Character.GetFaction`
- **Risks:** MonsterAI.UpdateAI is long and changes between versions: skip it only in the keep-away and rout branches and keep the base update through a reverse patch. TruePassiveMobs (prefix that skips it), FearMe (transpiler) and Odin's Ótti patch the same method; creature overhauls change damage and flee fields (the live check reads them anyway). The weak check is an estimate: it ignores resistances and damage over time unless those are published too. Hunting boars and necks becomes a chase once the player outclasses them. A creature that ignores the player can still target buildings (m_attackPlayerObjects). Nulling the player in FindEnemy also hides that player's tames for that search. Only MonsterAI creatures are covered; AnimalAI creatures already flee from their target. Sneak XP: weak creatures stay unalerted near an outclassing player, so crawling near them keeps paying the full vanilla 1 XP/s, where vanilla drops to 0.1 once a creature in range is alerted; option: a BaseAI.InStealthRange prefix on the sneaking player's client leaves out creatures weak against that player (their default items exist on every client, so it can run the same check). Training dummies must stay excluded: they are MonsterAI creatures whose attacks deal 1 damage (wiki, unverified), so every dummy would count as weak against every player. Mixed installs: a vanilla owner runs vanilla AI and ignores the rout RPC; a player without the mod publishes no strength. Ownership can change during a rout, hence the ZDO keys. The keep-away scan must be throttled (AI ticks run at 20 Hz for many creatures).
- **Related:** Sneak revamp; Training dummies revamp; Balista revamp
- **Game systems:** [docs/game/combat.md](game/combat.md)
- **Existing mods:** partial

  | Mod | Status | Notes |
  |---|---|---|
  | [TruePassiveMobs (lhoffl)](https://thunderstore.io/c/valheim/p/lhoffl/TruePassiveMobs/) | v1.1.0, updated 2026-09-28, 1.0: yes | Passive creatures (Lox, Asksvin, Moose, Boar, Hen, Neck, Bjorn by default) ignore players until damaged, then fight back for a while; skittish and territorial variants. Enemies flee from a player whose gear outclasses them (their best hit after your armor and resistances is at most 10% of your max HP, and/or you kill them in 3 hits or fewer); attacked enemies fight back; raid creatures never flee. Stores each player's combat profile in the player ZDO and prefix-skips MonsterAI.UpdateAI with a BaseAI.UpdateAI reverse patch. Needs Jotunn; required on the server and every client. MIT source: lhoffl/TruePassiveMobs. About 200 downloads. |
  | [FearMe (tulivu)](https://thunderstore.io/c/valheim/p/tulivu/FearMe/) | v1.0.1, updated 2026-09-19, 1.0: yes | Enemies compare your equipped gear tier (per-biome config) with their own: Cautious ignores you (BaseAI.FindEnemy postfix returns null), Afraid flees (a transpiler adds a flee branch just before the m_fleeIfHurtWhenTargetCantBeReached check in MonsterAI.UpdateAI). MIT source: tulivu/ValheimMods. About 11K downloads. |
  | [Odin's Ótti (DrakosDJ)](https://thunderstore.io/c/valheim/p/DrakosDJ/OdinsOtti/) | v1.0.1, updated 2026-09-12, 1.0: likely | Outmatched or outnumbered enemies calmly path around your party. Party threat from armor, weapon, skills, food and buffs plus nearby players, tames and summons; pack courage from the combined HP of allies within 20 m, bonus near their spawner. Hitting one provokes allies within 25 m, who then fight to the death. Crouching hides your threat. Tagged AI Generated, about 200 downloads, no public source found. |
  | [CowardlyGreydwarfs (omnipeach)](https://thunderstore.io/c/valheim/p/omnipeach/CowardlyGreydwarfs/) | v1.0.0, updated 2026-09-22, 1.0: yes | Greylings and greydwarfs flee after a heavy hit or at low health and come back when they feel strong enough. About 60 downloads. |
  | [FleeOnSight (Revel, Nexus 2764)](https://thunderstore.io/c/valheim/p/Revel/FleeOnSight/) | v1.1.1, updated 2024-05-11, 1.0: unknown | Listed creatures flee as soon as they are alerted once a boss is dead (global key): Greylings after Eikthyr, Greydwarfs after the Elder by default; option to keep fighting during raids. |
  | [Monster AI Tweaks (Nexus 758)](https://www.nexusmods.com/valheim/mods/758) | v0.4.0 (Thunderstore), updated 2022-11-05, 1.0: unknown (Nexus search snippets show activity into 2026) | Per-monster targeting, alertness, fire fear, sight and hearing. The Nexus version adds flee and chase by diet and, per a Nexus snippet, small groups (three or fewer) fight a larger threat until one is left, which then flees. |
  | [The Mark of Oden (PicSoul)](https://github.com/PicSoul/TheMarkOfOden) | v0.1.0, updated 2026-09-16, 1.0: dead (Thunderstore package PICS0UL/TheMarkOfOden deprecated; the GitHub source is at 0.4.0, pushed 2026-09-24, not released) | Threat from bosses you helped kill and species kill count, not gear. In the 0.4.0 source a creature you outrank leaves you alone (neither attacks nor runs); hit it and it fights back until, badly hurt, its nerve breaks and it flees, sooner as its packmates fall. Hunted animals (boar, neck, wolf, lox) may leave you alone but never break once provoked. Night courage, raid and boss-add exemptions, nameplate states (wary, fleeing, provoked, unafraid). MIT. The Thunderstore upload is tagged AI Generated. |

- **Inspiration:** Vanilla's only 'this player is too strong' rule is the Crown of Valheim: non-boss creatures that target a player in crown mode flee (MonsterAI.m_crownFearRange). The other flee settings ignore who the player is, and creatures have no leader or group link. Lines 1-2 are now well covered: TruePassiveMobs does almost exactly them (borrow its 'can it hurt you' test and its player profile in the player ZDO), FearMe adds a Cautious state (ignore without fleeing), Odin's Ótti a pack-courage model, and The Mark of Oden's unreleased 0.4.0 source leaves outranked players alone by progression rather than gear. Ours follows the sheet's gear wording ('fully geared from Ashlands'): the creature's best hit against the player's real armor and max HP, not Mark of Oden's progression model. Nobody routs a pack when its leader dies (the closest are Mark of Oden's 'breaks sooner as its packmates fall' and Odin's Ótti's pack courage): that is ours (kill a Troll, a Greydwarf Shaman or a Greydwarf Brute and the greydwarfs around it flee for a while). Ours also keeps weak creatures calm (walk away while they sense the player, fight back when hit or cornered). Option: recommend TruePassiveMobs for lines 1-2 and build only the rout, or build both in one mod since they share the flee plumbing. All logic must run on whichever client owns each creature.

### Training dummies revamp

> Merge with "More training dummies" Make that dummies can target any hostile creature in range Make the dummies toggleable ON and OFF (OFF state makes the dummy completely inactive and it attacks nothing) Create new dummies for each type of weapons (currently only sword and shield with throwable rock as a range attack)

- **Scope:** Revamp · **Feasibility:** medium · **Who needs it:** Both · **Custom assets:** no · **Status:** idea
- **Approach:** Merge: this idea absorbs More training dummies (cancelled in the idea sheet); the per-weapon dummies are the new dummies, and the older utility variants stay optional extras, listed under More training dummies. ON works as in vanilla, plus hostile creatures; OFF does nothing (decided by the user, 2026-09-29). The vanilla T.W.I.G., once alerted (it sees a player in its front half within its 10 m alert range x the player's stealth factor, or it is hit), attacks that player for 1 damage per attack (wiki); from the code it also attacks tames, because the tamed rule of BaseAI.IsEnemy runs before the faction table (not tested; the wiki says it only attacks players); its hits land only on characters that IsEnemy accepts, so in vanilla they never hurt wild creatures, and no creature picks it on its own (m_aiSkipTarget). An ON dummy keeps all of that and also fights hostile creatures in range: the creature rules (alert range, damage) apply only when the target is a hostile creature, so players and tames meet the vanilla dummy, and the optional settings default to vanilla. It still attacks the closest enemy it senses (BaseAI.FindEnemy), which can now be a creature, and after a creature fight it stays alerted, so it sees all around, until MonsterAI.UpdateTarget drops the alert (30 s without sensing a target or 60 s without attacking), as after a fight with a player. An OFF dummy attacks nothing. Fight hostile creatures: postfix BaseAI.IsEnemy so a TrainingDummy-faction character that is ON treats hostile creatures (not a player, not tamed, has MonsterAI, faction not Players, PlayerSpawned or TrainingDummy, Dverger only when aggravated) as enemies; checking the faction first keeps this hot path cheap. The same rule makes its swings, throw and AoE land, because non-player attackers only hit characters IsEnemy accepts (Attack.DoMeleeAttack, Attack.DoAreaAttack, Projectile.IsValidTarget, Aoe.ShouldHit). Tames keep the vanilla behaviour: the tamed rule already makes them enemies of the dummy, and an ON dummy keeps attacking them, with no setting to spare them; since a tame never picks a dummy itself (m_aiSkipTarget), Combat pet sparring starts when a dummy hits the tame. The dummy cannot walk (m_speed 0): it sees 30 m up to 90 degrees off its facing (all around once alerted) and hears nothing, but MonsterAI.UpdateAI only attacks once it is alerted, and sight alerts it only within m_alertRange (10 m) or when it is hit; so 'in range' needs a longer alert range for creatures (per-dummy config) that lets the thrown attack engage at range, while melee still needs each attack's reach. Players and tames keep the vanilla 10 m: m_alertRange is read only in MonsterAI.UpdateAI and MonsterAI.UpdateTarget, so a MonsterAI.UpdateTarget postfix (called from UpdateAI before the alert check) sets it on each call, to the creature range while the target is a hostile creature and to the prefab value otherwise. Every vanilla attack deals 1 damage (wiki): a Character.Damage prefix on the dummy's owner, before RPC_Damage, sets hits from dummies on hostile creatures to a per-dummy config value, so players and tames keep the vanilla training damage. Creatures still never pick a dummy on their own (m_aiSkipTarget), but MonsterAI.OnDamaged makes a creature it hits fight back when that creature has no target yet, so dummies draw creatures that are not already busy with a player. ON/OFF: an Interactable component added to every dummy prefab (ZNetScene.Awake) and a Character.GetHoverText postfix; E, gated by PrivateArea.CheckAccess, sends an RPC to the owner (ZNetView.InvokeRPC), which flips a ZDO bool, default ON. When OFF, a MonsterAI.UpdateAI prefix clears m_targetCreature and m_targetStatic and calls SetAlerted(false), a MonsterAI.UpdateTarget prefix skips the search, a MonsterAI.OnDamaged prefix keeps training hits from alerting it, BaseAI.UpdateAI still regenerates the dummy so it can still be hit for training, and a BaseAI.DoIdleSound prefix mutes it on every client. Per weapon type: clone piece_TrainingDummy for knives, clubs and maces, axes, two-handed axes and sledges, spears, polearms, fists, bows, crossbows and staffs (the vanilla T.W.I.G. is the sword and shield one); each clone gets cloned TrainingDummy_attack, _attack2, _attack3 and TrainingDummy_throw items with the weapon's damage type at the T.W.I.G.'s training damage (m_damages; hits on hostile creatures use the per-dummy value), reach (m_aiAttackRange, Attack.m_attackRange), rhythm (m_aiAttackInterval) and projectile (Attack.m_attackProjectile: a vanilla arrow, bolt, thrown spear or staff projectile on the throw animation) as Humanoid.m_defaultItems, a vanilla weapon model on the dummy's hand, its own name, description and recipe, registered in ZNetScene, ObjectDB and the Hammer's PieceTable. The dummy's own animations are reused; weapon-specific moves would need new clips in an AssetBundle. Ships as Both (house rule): the server refuses players without the mod and sends its settings to everyone.
- **Hooks:** `BaseAI.IsEnemy`, `BaseAI.FindEnemy`, `MonsterAI.UpdateAI`, `MonsterAI.UpdateTarget`, `MonsterAI.OnDamaged`, `MonsterAI.m_alertRange`, `BaseAI.DoIdleSound`, `BaseAI.UpdateRegeneration`, `Character.Damage`, `Character.GetHoverText`, `Character.m_aiSkipTarget`, `ZNetScene.Awake`, `ObjectDB.Awake`, `ZNetView.InvokeRPC`, `PrivateArea.CheckAccess`, `Humanoid.m_defaultItems`, `Humanoid.EquipBestWeapon`, `Attack.DoMeleeAttack`, `Projectile.IsValidTarget`, `Aoe.ShouldHit`, `PieceTable.m_pieces`, `CharacterDrop.OnDeath`
- **Risks:** BaseAI.IsEnemy runs for every character in every AI search: branch on the faction first. A 2,500 HP decoy that heals fully in 30 s and that creatures fight back makes dummy walls a base-defence exploit: cap damage to creatures, optionally pause regeneration for a few seconds after a creature hit (BaseAI.UpdateRegeneration; not BaseAI.m_timeSinceHurt, which player hits reset too, so training keeps the vanilla regeneration) and limit crowding. Loot drops whoever kills (CharacterDrop.OnDeath has no killer check, and a ragdoll that drops the items drops them instead), so dummies next to spawners farm trophies unattended: offer no drops for dummy kills (on the victim's owner). Dummies killed by creatures refund their materials (Character.ApplyDamage calls Piece.DropResources). Ballistas with no trophy set shoot dummies, in vanilla too (BaseAI.FindClosestCreature ignores m_aiSkipTarget); the Balista revamp skips them. Other MC ideas must ignore dummies: hitting one pays vanilla adrenaline, since it is an enemy of players (Attack.DoMeleeAttack: m_attackAdrenaline x its m_enemyAdrenalineMultiplier, prefab value unverified), so the Adrenaline revamp ignores Faction.TrainingDummy for combat state, engaged enemies and the kill bonus (weapon hits on a dummy still pay, as in vanilla, so a trinket can be tried out on one; with no decay, a bar filled there is kept for the next fight); dummies run MonsterAI and hit players for 1, so the Mob AI revamp never affects them (its weakness test would otherwise stop them attacking players); an OFF dummy is never alerted, so every hit on it is a backstab (Character.RPC_Damage, once per 300 s per dummy) and sneaking near it pays full vanilla Sneak XP (BaseAI.InStealthRange), so the Sneak revamp gives no sneak-attack XP for dummy victims. A dummy owned by a client without the mod ignores the OFF flag and the switch RPC is dropped, so every player needs the mod. New dummy prefab names are saved in ZDOs: permanent, and the dummies do not appear while the mod is missing. Reused sword animations make a spear or bow dummy look off; whether the built-in sword and shield can be hidden and whether the dummy turns in place are unverified. TouchGrass uses the same interact key and moves dummies at night. Prefab facts come from a third-party dump: check them at runtime.
- **Related:** More training dummies; Balista revamp; Mob AI revamp; Combat pet; Adrenaline revamp; Sneak revamp
- **Game systems:** [docs/game/combat.md](game/combat.md)
- **Existing mods:** none

  | Mod | Status | Notes |

  | [TouchGrass (sighsorry)](https://thunderstore.io/c/valheim/p/sighsorry/TouchGrass/) | v1.0.7, updated 2026-09-10, 1.0: yes (tagged Deep North Update) | Using a dummy opens a window that sets that dummy's damage amount and type; config for dummy health and recipe; optional night aggro that slides dummies toward players within 16 m (they cannot walk); crowding limit; DPS and XP meter; stationary skill fatigue. The opposite direction of this idea. Tagged AI Generated, about 1.9K downloads. |
  | [OdinTrainingPlace (OdinPlus)](https://thunderstore.io/c/valheim/p/OdinPlus/OdinTrainingPlace/) | v1.6.6, updated 2026-09-19, 1.0: yes | Wooden dummy and a mechanical dummy for blocking practice among other training pieces; none of them attacks creatures. About 248K downloads. |
  | [DPS (JereKuusela)](https://thunderstore.io/c/valheim/p/JereKuusela/DPS/) | v1.7.0, updated 2026-09-10, 1.0: likely | Console commands to spawn, reset and kill dummies with configurable resistances and status effects. A test tool, not a gameplay feature. |

- **Inspiration:** Vanilla T.W.I.G. (ServersideQoL's 1.0 component dump and the wiki): piece_TrainingDummy is itself a Humanoid with MonsterAI and Piece, 2,500 HP, full regeneration in 30 s, cannot walk, sees 30 m up to 90 degrees off its facing, hears nothing and only attacks once alerted (10 m alert range), m_aiSkipTarget true so creatures never pick it on their own, and every attack deals 1 damage. There is no prior art for dummies that fight creatures, can be switched off or wield other weapons; TouchGrass only goes the other way (dummies hunt players at night) and lets you change one dummy's damage type. One faction rule (BaseAI.IsEnemy) and a longer alert range used against creatures alone make dummies fight hostile creatures, while players and tames still meet the vanilla dummy; creatures never pick a dummy on their own (m_aiSkipTarget) and only fight back when a dummy hits them while they have no target. Borrow TouchGrass's per-dummy settings on interaction (on the alternate key, so E stays the ON/OFF switch; they default to vanilla) and its crowding limit, which also stops dummy walls. Watch base-defence balance, loot farms (a creature killed by a dummy drops its loot) and TouchGrass using the same key.

### Weapon revamp

> Objective is a SLIGHT rework of the vanilla weapon gameplay MAYBE rework the existing animations/moveset to be a bit more dynamic Add a roll attack (roll then attack) Add a parry attack (parry then attack) Add a jump attack (jump then attack)

- **Scope:** Revamp · **Feasibility:** medium · **Who needs it:** Depends · **Custom assets:** no · **Status:** [Weapon Moveset 0.1.0](../src/Combat/Weapons.Moveset) (in development)
- **Approach:** Vanilla today: Humanoid.StartAttack refuses to start while dodging, staggering, knocked back or in a minor action, but has no ground check, so you can already swing in the air (Humanoid.GetAttackSpeedFactorMovement returns 1 while airborne, so the jump keeps its momentum). Player.PlayerAttackInput buffers a press for 0.5 s and retries StartAttack every FixedUpdate, so an attack pressed late in a roll starts as soon as the roll ends, and Player.UpdateDodge refuses a roll during an attack. A held parry staggers an attacker that has m_staggerWhenBlocked, and Character.RPC_Damage then doubles damage on that non-player. So the three moments exist; they just play the normal swing. The mod turns them into distinct moves. Detection, all on the local player, each with a short configurable window: (1) Roll attack: a Player.UpdateDodge postfix stamps the moment m_inDodge turns false, and a primary attack within about 0.4 s (example value) counts; the vanilla buffer already covers a press made in the last 0.5 s of the roll. (2) Parry attack: a Humanoid.BlockAttack prefix runs the vanilla parry test (m_timedBlockBonus above 1 and the private m_blockTimer between 0 and 0.25 s) and opens a scope that a finalizer closes. On a parry, vanilla calls Player.AddAdrenaline only when the block held (stamina left, no stagger from the hit) and there was an attacker, whatever the amount (even 0, and also without a trinket), so an AddAdrenaline call inside the scope marks a held parry exactly; testing HaveStamina and Character.GetStaggerPercentage in a postfix is close, but it sees the stamina after the parry's own stamina change. The Adrenaline revamp needs the same test to tell a parry from a block (m_timedBlockBonus > 1, 0 <= m_blockTimer < 0.25, and an AddAdrenaline call inside that scope), so write it once and share it. Catch the call with a Priority.First prefix or a postfix on Player.AddAdrenaline, so the detector still fires when another prefix (the Adrenaline revamp's, GrindstoneSkills') rewrites the amount. A held parry opens a window of about 1 s (PPR uses 1 s, GrindstoneSkills 2 s). (3) Jump attack: a Character.Jump prefix marks jumps from input (force false; animation events call Jump(force: true)), Player.OnJump confirms the jump happened, and a primary attack before Character.IsOnGround is true again counts; walking off a ledge does not (GCO accepts it after 1 s of airtime, optional). Execution through vanilla code: a Humanoid.StartAttack prefix for Player.m_localPlayer, primary attacks of melee weapons only (Attack.AttackType Horizontal, Vertical or Area; bows, staves and m_requiresReload weapons keep vanilla; so do weapons whose m_skillType is Blocking, such as the Better tower shields bash, a clone of the unarmed combo that may pass the type test (prefab, unverified): a roll or jump move would replace the bash and stack its x2 stagger on the bash's own x5 to x10), sets a pending-move flag that a finalizer clears. An Attack.Start prefix then edits the per-swing clone that StartAttack just made (Attack.Clone is a MemberwiseClone, so the item's SharedData stays untouched): m_attackAnimation becomes the move's trigger, with m_attackChainLevels and m_attackRandomAnimations at 0 so Start fires that name as is, plus m_damageMultiplier, m_staggerMultiplier, m_forceMultiplier, m_attackStamina and m_attackAdrenaline. The stamina check, rotation and other mods' patches run unchanged, and because the name differs from the weapon's own, the next normal swing restarts the combo at its first hit (as GCO's jump attacks do). Animations come from a per-family table (skill type and m_animationState) that defaults to the family's own triggers. A chained attack fires m_attackAnimation plus the chain index, so the finisher can be played directly: roll attack = the finisher, a lunging heavy hit out of the roll; parry attack = the first chain hit with a faster wind-up (animator speed through CharacterAnimEvent.Speed), a quick riposte; jump attack = the finisher or the secondary animation with extra stagger, optionally with a downward pull and a small area slam on landing (Character.m_onLand only fires after a drop of more than 0.8 m from the highest point, so a short jump may not reach it: fall back to the first IsOnGround after the attack). Check every trigger with ZSyncAnimation.HasParameter at start-up and fall back to the weapon's secondary animation; trigger names such as swing_longsword, atgeir_attack, greatsword_secondary or dualaxes come from mod READMEs (unverified), so dump every weapon's m_attackAnimation and chain levels at runtime. Sword Heavy Slash already plays dualaxes0 and greatsword2 on swords, so borrowing another family's trigger works. The vanilla finisher bonus (x2 damage and x1.2 push in Attack.DoMeleeAttack) needs more than one chain level, so the moves use m_damageMultiplier instead; starting values to tune: roll attack x1.3 damage, parry attack x1.5 damage and x2 stagger, jump attack x1.2 damage and x2 stagger. A normal combo already ends on that x2 finisher, so a move that plays the finisher animation at x1.3 hits less hard than the vanilla finisher: tune against it. Adrenaline: vanilla pays the clone's m_attackAdrenaline per enemy hit (times the victim's m_enemyAdrenalineMultiplier, Attack.DoMeleeAttack), so that field is the per-move bonus. The Adrenaline revamp can pay a per-move bonus for them; the moves cost no adrenaline, because the bar is kept for the trinket, which the player triggers (Trinket revamp). It does not time these moves (their m_attackAnimation differs from the weapon's own) and pays the weapon's learned amount x the clone's m_attackAdrenaline / the weapon's. A move that borrows the secondary animation stays untimed too, because the Adrenaline revamp compares it with the primary attack it was cloned from. A parry attack on a family without a chain (m_attackChainLevels 1 or less, prefab data) would keep the vanilla trigger and be timed as a normal swing with no per-move bonus: give it another trigger. Livelier swings (the 'maybe' line, optional and mild by default): raise the clone's m_speedFactor and m_speedFactorRotation (code default 0.2) for movement and turning while swinging, and patch Humanoid.OnStopMoving too, because the clip's Stop event sets both to 0 on the current attack; scale the forward root motion in a Character.AddRootMotion prefix while attacking for a longer lunge (root motion only accumulates during a dodge, attack or emote, and Character.ApplyRootMotion uses it when it is larger than the input velocity); scale CharacterAnimEvent.Speed during wind-up or recovery for tempo; optionally let a block or roll cut the recovery after the hit, which vanilla forbids (Humanoid.IsBlocking is false and Player.UpdateDodge refuses while InAttack) and which needs an Animator exit (unverified), as AttackCancel and GCO's block canceling do. Everything runs on the attacker's client, the hit only uses vanilla HitData fields that a vanilla victim owner applies, triggers reach everyone through ZSyncAnimation.SetTrigger, and the owner's animator speed is mirrored through the ZDO: technically client-only. It changes combat balance, so it ships as Both: the server refuses players without the mod and sends its settings to everyone. Only new animation clips would need an AssetBundle and an AnimatorOverrideController on every client.
- **Hooks:** `Humanoid.StartAttack`, `Attack.Start`, `Attack.OnAttackTrigger`, `Player.PlayerAttackInput`, `Player.UpdateDodge`, `Player.OnDodgeMortal`, `Character.Jump`, `Player.OnJump`, `Character.IsOnGround`, `Humanoid.BlockAttack`, `Player.AddAdrenaline`, `Character.GetStaggerPercentage`, `Humanoid.GetAttackSpeedFactorMovement`, `Humanoid.GetAttackSpeedFactorRotation`, `Humanoid.OnStopMoving`, `Character.AddRootMotion`, `CharacterAnimEvent.Speed`, `ZSyncAnimation.HasParameter`
- **Risks:** A trigger missing from the Animator does nothing, silently, and one that exists but has no transition from the current state (idle, dodge, jump or fall) may fire late or never (Animator data, unverified): test every weapon family. Borrowed clips can clip through a shield or change the hit timing (the hit fires on the clip's Hit event), while the hit geometry (m_attackRange, m_attackAngle, m_attackHeight) stays the weapon's. Cancel Animation Cancels, for unarmed, spears, axes, battleaxes and atgeirs, clears the queued attacks when a dodge starts right after an attack and suppresses the primary attack while block and attack are held during a dodge (the default keyboard roll is block + jump), so a roll attack pressed mid-roll with block still held is lost for those families; the koumodgp anti-cheat lists animation cancels among the exploits it guards against: detect both and warn. Other mods own the same inputs: GCO (running and jump attacks on primary; turn our jump attack off when it is present), PPR (counter on the secondary attack after a parry), GrindstoneSkills Riposte (a damage bonus that stacks with ours), SpecialAttack, SecondaryAttacks and Quickstep (a dash may not set the dodge tag the roll detector watches, unverified), AttackCancel and AttackCancleCounter. The Dual wielding idea edits the same Attack.Start clone for a pair of one-handed weapons (vanilla dual-axe or dual-knife triggers): run it first, and pick the context moves from the pair's set. Balance: roll then boosted hit can be spammed behind the roll's i-frames (charge stamina or add a short cooldown), the jump attack pays jump plus attack stamina, and the parry attack stacks with the vanilla x2 on staggered targets; the server settings (the mod ships as Both) keep the values equal for everyone, PvP included. Player.AddAdrenaline is shared: the Adrenaline revamp, GrindstoneSkills and AdrenalineModifier patch it with prefixes that rewrite amounts, so the parry detector reads the call, not the amount (Priority.First or a postfix); test the parry attack with the Adrenaline revamp on. A mod that turns on the vanilla block-charge counter (CaptainValheim, ZenCombat) fires Attack.StartWithoutAnimation inside BlockAttack, and the hits of that counter call AddAdrenaline (Attack.DoMeleeAttack), so a parry that did not hold would look held and the Adrenaline revamp would tag those hit gains as block or parry: the shared helper ignores calls made during Attack.StartWithoutAnimation (a flag set in its prefix, cleared in a finalizer). A plunge's speed does not raise fall damage (Character.UpdateGroundContact uses the height fallen), but a fall above 4 m still hurts. StartAttack also runs for monsters, so act only for Player.m_localPlayer. MC Crossbow Stays Loaded patches Attack.OnAttackTrigger and Humanoid.BlockAttack (both postfixes; crossbows are excluded; cross test). EpicLoot and weapon packs such as Therzie's Warfare (which has its own animation replacement) patch Attack and Humanoid. Optional new clips need an AssetBundle and override pipeline that clashes with other animation mods, and players without the mod would see the vanilla clips.
- **Related:** Adrenaline revamp; Better tower shields; Dual wielding
- **Game systems:** [docs/game/combat.md](game/combat.md)
- **Existing mods:** partial

Its README lists the vanilla attack animation names from before 1.0 (for example swing_longsword, atgeir_attack, greatsword, greatsword_secondary, unarmed_attack, unarmed_kick; unverified for 1.0). - **Inspiration:** Borrow GCO's input rules for the jump attack (primary before landing after a deliberate jump, a second of airtime when stepping off a ledge) and its choice to borrow the family's finisher without advancing the combo; PPR's short counter window after a parry and GrindstoneSkills' 2 s riposte window; and Sword Heavy Slash's proof that other families' triggers play on a weapon. What they get wrong: GCO only comes as a full overhaul (stamina, hyperarmor, PvP, enemy behaviour); PPR puts its counter on the secondary attack; GrindstoneSkills and Combat Momentum only add numbers; SpecialAttack and SecondaryAttacks add specials on the secondary slot or new keys; nobody has a roll attack, and Cancel Animation Cancels even suppresses some attacks queued through a roll for some weapons. Ours: a slight, standalone layer on the vanilla moveset. The primary attack becomes a roll attack, a parry attack or a jump attack when started in a short window after that action, each with its own vanilla animation and a modest bonus, while the secondary attack keeps its vanilla role. Optional light swing tuning (movement while swinging, lunge, tempo) stays mild by default. Technically client-only with vanilla animations, shipped as Both (server settings for everyone), a per-move adrenaline bonus the Adrenaline revamp can pay (the moves cost no adrenaline), and detection of GCO, PPR, Cancel Animation Cancels and the quickstep mods.

### More training dummies

- **Scope:** New · **Feasibility:** easy · **Who needs it:** Both · **Custom assets:** no · **Status:** cancelled
- **Approach:** Cancelled in the idea sheet: merged into the Training dummies revamp. The per-weapon dummies planned there are the new dummies. What stays here are optional utility variants the revamp does not ask for, built as more clones of piece_TrainingDummy in the same mod: a resistance or weakness dummy (configurable m_damageModifiers), a parry trainer that swings on a fixed, telegraphed rhythm (cloned attack items with a set m_aiAttackInterval as Humanoid.m_defaultItems), a DPS readout from m_onDamaged (TouchGrass and DPS already have one), a moving archery target with ArcheryTarget scoring, and a tall dummy.

- **Risks:** Same as Training dummies revamp: dummies are Characters (IsEnemy hot path, AI and character loops) and refund their materials when killed (Character.ApplyDamage calls Piece.DropResources).

### Blood trinket

> Trinket that, when it procs, sets you to 15% HP for 10 seconds then heals you quickly,

- **Scope:** New · **Feasibility:** medium · **Who needs it:** Both · **Custom assets:** yes · **Status:** idea
- **Approach:** Item: a new trinket cloned from a vanilla trinket prefab (for example TrinketBloodGoldHealth). It gets its own name, icon, m_maxAdrenaline (config, starting value 70, a bit above the 60-cost trinket the Adrenaline revamp tunes against, so its bar fills a little less than once per fight; balance unverified) and a proposed Ashlands-tier recipe around Bloodstone (GemstoneRed); recipe and station are a design choice to test. The Trinket revamp keeps the vanilla costs, so this config is the Blood trinket's own cost. Clear m_equipStatusEffect on the clone, in case the source trinket carries one (prefab data, or another mod's passive): the Blood trinket has no passive. It is registered in ObjectDB and ZNetScene; everyone needs it (a new item), and it ships as Both: the server refuses players without the mod and sends its settings to everyone. Its m_fullAdrenalineSE is our 'blood pact' StatusEffect subclass. Trigger: when the player triggers the trinket (the Trinket revamp's key, with a full bar), the vanilla pop in Player.AddAdrenaline adds that SE on the local player (SEMan.AddStatusEffect clones it and calls Setup). The Blood trinket needs the Trinket revamp (ModRequires), so the drop never comes at a moment the player did not choose: without it, vanilla would pop the trinket as soon as the bar fills. Setup records the current HP and, if it is above 15% of max, sets it to 15% with Character.SetHealth (owner only). That is not a hit: no armor, no shield, no adrenaline penalty, and HP never reaches 0. Nor is it a blood-magic payment (Character.SetHealth, not Character.UseHealth), so the Blood magic XP idea pays no XP for it. Setup stores the amount taken (HP before minus HP after; 0 when HP was already at or below 15%) in a value field of the clone, for the heal-back. This is the only time the trinket sets HP; it never holds HP down afterwards. The 10 s window: heals work and you can die, as decided by the user (2026-09-29). Nothing is clamped: food regen (Player.UpdateFood, every 10 s), meads, other heals and the Blood stone revamp's lifesteal raise HP as usual, and each heal lowers the bloodstone bonus, so healing out early is the player's choice. Damage still counts, so a big hit kills; there is no 1 HP floor or other protection. Heal-back: after the window, UpdateStatusEffect heals the stored amount in ticks with Character.Heal, over a short time (config, starting value 3 s); the last tick pays what rounding left. This works like the vanilla heal over time in SE_Stats (SE_Stats.Setup splits m_healthOverTime into ticks of m_healthOverTimeInterval, and SE_Stats.UpdateStatusEffect calls Character.Heal per tick), but those fields do not fit as they are: their ticks are counted from Setup, so they would heal during the window instead of after it. The heal-back is the full amount taken, whatever heals came in the window; Character.RPC_Heal caps each tick at the current max HP, so a player who is already full, or healed to full in the window, gets nothing more, and HP never goes above max. m_ttl is 10 s plus the heal time, so the base IsDone ends the effect and its icon counts down both phases; SEMan.Update calls UpdateStatusEffect before IsDone, so a last tick paid when the time runs out still lands. Death in the window or during the heal-back: Player.OnDeath calls SEMan.RemoveAllStatusEffects, which stops the effect, and the rest of the heal-back is dropped (Stop heals nothing, and Character.RPC_Heal refuses a heal at 0 HP anyway); the respawn starts at full HP (Player.Load turns a saved HP of 0 into max). Logout: status effects are not saved (SEMan has no save code, and Player.Save writes the current HP but no status effect), so the rest of the heal-back is lost and the player comes back at the saved HP; a mead's effect is lost the same way. Carrying it over (the amount in Player.m_customData, which Player.Save writes, paid after the next spawn) is possible but not planned. ResetTime is overridden to do nothing, so a second trigger during the window or the heal-back does not restart the timer (the trigger runs the vanilla pop, which calls ResetTime on a running full-adrenaline effect and still empties the bar); HP is not dropped again either, because the drop is in Setup, which runs only when the effect is added. Payoff: bloodstone weapons gain 1 + missing HP x m_damageMultiplierPerMissingHP in Attack.ModifyDamage (wiki 0.2% per HP, so +34% at 200 max HP with 15% left). Blood-magic costs are a share of current HP, and m_staminaReturnPerMissingHP refunds stamina. An optional bonus of its own during the window (for example more damage, or lifesteal, which works because nothing is clamped) is a decision for the user.
- **Hooks:** `ObjectDB.Awake`, `ObjectDB.CopyOtherDB`, `ZNetScene.Awake`, `Player.AddAdrenaline`, `StatusEffect.Setup`, `StatusEffect.UpdateStatusEffect`, `StatusEffect.ResetTime`, `StatusEffect.IsDone`, `StatusEffect.Stop`, `Character.SetHealth`, `Character.GetMaxHealth`, `Character.Heal`, `Attack.ModifyDamage`, `Humanoid.EquipItem`, `ItemDrop.ItemData.GetTooltip`
- **Risks:** Death risk by design: no 1 HP floor. GrindstoneSkills (Desperation below 25% HP, Last Stand), UndyingAmulet (cheat death) and EpicLoot's low-health effects change the balance. Character.SetHealth only writes on the owner; the pop runs there, but never call it for other players. StatusEffect.Clone is a shallow copy, so keep per-instance state (amount taken, heal-back paid so far) in value fields. New-item risks: uninstalling deletes the item, a player without the mod loses it (drops, chests, the trinket visual through VisEquipment), and the prefab name, recipe and station must be checked at runtime. How often the bar fills comes from the adrenaline system, so tune the cost with the Adrenaline revamp. With extra trinket slots (MultiTrinket), one trigger fires it together with the other equipped trinkets. In PvP, 15% HP is an easy kill until the player heals. It overlaps the Blood stone revamp's blood rite (pay HP, no regen, optional last-stand ward). Nothing is clamped in the window, so that idea's lifesteal lifts HP there (and lowers the bloodstone bonus). The rite's no-regen option (m_healthRegenMultiplier = 0) stops only the food regen tick in Player.UpdateFood, not Character.Heal, so the heal-back still lands during a rite. The trinket adds no Character.ApplyDamage prefix: if a rite is active in the window, only the rite's optional ward can catch a lethal hit. Design them together. Ships as Both, so the server's settings apply to everyone.
- **Related:** Trinket revamp; Adrenaline revamp; Blood stone revamp; Blood magic XP
- **Game systems:** [docs/game/combat.md](game/combat.md)
- **Existing mods:** none

'Desperation' doubles damage reduction below 25% health. 'Last Stand' (level 100) leaves you at 1 HP and untouchable for 2 s on a killing blow. Would soften our window. |
  | [UndyingAmulet (Gamesmodding)](https://thunderstore.io/c/valheim/p/Gamesmodding/UndyingAmulet/) | v1.1.1, updated 2026-09-27, 1.0: yes (needs Valheim 1.0.15 or newer) | Cheat death from the inventory: a lethal hit restores 50% health with 30 s of immunity, on a 30 min cooldown. While it is ready, it also gives +50% sprint speed, half stamina costs and 20% lifesteal. Needs Jotunn. AI Generated tag, about 130 downloads. |

Blood shardstones such as Bloodrage (from the Blood stone revamp research). About 2.26M downloads. |

- **Inspiration:** No trinket or item was found that sets the player's HP low on purpose for a timed window. Vanilla already rewards low HP with bloodstone weapons (Attack.ModifyDamage, m_damageMultiplierPerMissingHP, wiki 0.2% per missing HP), and blood-magic costs are a share of current HP. Going low today means casting a blood staff or taking hits. Borrow EpicLoot's 30% low-health threshold as a reference point. GrindstoneSkills' and UndyingAmulet's low-HP safety nets are not borrowed: the window has no 1 HP floor, so a big hit kills. Ours is a trinket that, when the player triggers it, makes the bloodstone bonus peak for up to 10 s (heals still work, so the player can end it sooner) and then gives the HP back over a few seconds, with no eitr and no weapon swap. It pairs with the Blood stone revamp's blood rite: nothing is clamped in the window, so that idea's lifesteal works there too. With the Trinket revamp, the player picks the moment of the drop, for example right before a bloodstone combo.

### Dual wielding

> Make so that one handed weapons can be dual wielded

- **Scope:** New · **Feasibility:** medium · **Who needs it:** Client · **Custom assets:** no · **Status:** [Dual Wielding 0.1.0](../src/Combat/Weapons.DualWield) (in development)
- **Approach:** Vanilla: Humanoid.EquipItem puts a OneHandedWeapon in the right hand and unequips a left item that is not a Shield or Torch, so two one-handed weapons never stay equipped. Attacks use Humanoid.GetCurrentWeapon (the right item first), and StartAttack clones that weapon's Attack and passes the weapon to Attack.Start. Blocking uses the private Humanoid.GetCurrentBlocker (the left item first), so a player without a shield already blocks and parries with a weapon. The vanilla dual weapons, the Berserkir axes (AxeBerzerkr) and Skoll and Hati (KnifeSkollAndHati), are single items whose attacks play the player's dual-axe and dual-knife animation sets (player_DualAxes.fbx and player_DualKnives.fbx in the SoftRef manifest). (1) Equip rules for the local player. Eligible weapons: OneHandedWeapon items with skill Swords, Axes, Clubs or Knives and a melee primary attack, minus a config exclusion list. Spears stay out, as in Smoothbrain's DualWield, because their secondary throws the weapon (DualWielder and balrond DualMastery let a spear keep its vanilla attacks instead). An eligible weapon equipped while the main hand holds one goes to the off hand (replacing the off-hand weapon). A key held when the player equips it picks the main hand instead, and a swap key swaps the pair. An eligible weapon equipped while only the off hand holds one goes to the main hand and keeps the pair (eating hides only the right hand and re-equips it). A shield keeps the vanilla rule; a torch replaces the off-hand weapon. An EquipItem prefix handles only these cases: it repeats the short vanilla guards and finishes like vanilla (m_equipped, clearing the hidden hand items, the private SetupEquipment and TriggerEquipEffect). The key is read in Player.ToggleEquipped, because items with m_equipDuration are equipped later by Player.UpdateActionQueue. (2) Keep the pair consistent. Move the off-hand weapon to the main hand when the main hand empties, but not inside EquipItem, UnequipAllItems (also the death path, Player.CreateTombStone) or HideHandItems (a depth counter). Restore the pair in order in ShowHandItems, because vanilla re-equips the left item first. An off-hand marker in ItemData.m_customData lets Player.EquipInventoryItems restore the hands on load. Turning the feature off unequips the off-hand weapon. (3) Stance: a SetupAnimationState postfix sets the m_animationState of the same vanilla dual item the attacks borrow (probably DualAxes for AxeBerzerkr; prefab data), synced like any stance. (4) Attacks: an Attack.Start prefix keeps the main-hand weapon's Attack and copies only m_attackAnimation, m_attackChainLevels and m_attackRandomAnimations from the vanilla dual item (AxeBerzerkr for any pair, KnifeSkollAndHati for two knives), read at runtime and checked with ZSyncAnimation.HasParameter, plus a stamina factor. The secondary is the dual item's leap by default. (5) Each Hit animation event runs Attack.OnAttackTrigger once, and DoMeleeAttack reads damage, status effect, backstab, tool tier, push, skill and durability from the clone's m_weapon. A prefix alternates m_weapon between the main and the off hand per event (restarting with the combo) and applies an off-hand damage factor through m_damageMultiplier; a postfix restores the main hand. (6) Blocking: vanilla already blocks and parries with the off-hand weapon; option to use the better of the two. (7) Cosmetics: left trails (a VisEquipment.SetWeaponTrails postfix) and a mirrored back joint (AttachBackItem puts every one-handed weapon on m_backMelee). Who needs it: technically client-only. The left item hash is in the player ZDO and every client draws it, the dual moves are vanilla triggers sent to everybody, the stance is a synced animator parameter, and hits are built by the attacker and resolved by the victim's owner; only the left trail and the back joint are per client. Under the house rule that experience-changing mods ship as Both, it ships as Both: the server refuses players without the mod and sends its settings to everyone.
- **Hooks:** `Humanoid.EquipItem`, `Player.ToggleEquipped`, `Humanoid.UnequipItem`, `Humanoid.UnequipAllItems`, `Humanoid.HideHandItems`, `Humanoid.ShowHandItems`, `Humanoid.SetupEquipment`, `Player.EquipInventoryItems`, `Humanoid.SetupAnimationState`, `Humanoid.SetAnimationState`, `Attack.Start`, `Attack.OnAttackTrigger`, `ZSyncAnimation.HasParameter`, `ObjectDB.GetItemPrefab`, `Humanoid.GetCurrentBlocker`, `Humanoid.GetCurrentWeapon`, `VisEquipment.SetWeaponTrails`, `VisEquipment.AttachBackItem`, `VisEquipment.Awake`
- **Risks:** Animator: the dual triggers and stance were made for the vanilla dual items. Whether each trigger leaves the dual stance, a jump or a dodge cleanly, and where each clip places its Hit events, is Animator data (unverified). The clips fit axes and knives: long swords and maces may clip through the body, and the hit geometry stays the main weapon's. The leap keeps the other fields of the main-hand secondary (attack type, range), which may not fit it (prefab data, unverified). The trigger names used by mods (dualaxes, dual_knives, dualaxes_secondary, dual_knives_secondary) are unverified, so read them from the vanilla items. Grip: a one-handed weapon's attach child is made for the right hand, and VisEquipment.AttachItem places it on the left hand joint with the same offset, so the blade may face the wrong way (unverified); neither DualWielder's nor Smoothbrain's source corrects the hand grip, and a correction only helps players with the mod. Balance: the dual-axe combo lands 6 hits in 4 steps (wiki) against 3 in a sword combo. Each hit carries its weapon's damage, a status effect roll, stagger, adrenaline and skill XP, there is no shield, and two axes chop a tree on every hit. Tune the off-hand factor, stamina and speed against the Berserkir axes and a sword and shield; the server settings (Both) keep them equal. Equipment edge cases: hide and show, eating, swimming, crafting stations, loading, death and tombstones (Inventory.MoveInventoryToGrave leaves equipped items on the player), the equip delay and the live toggle. Humanoid.EquipItem also runs for creatures (Humanoid.EquipBestWeapon), so act only for the local player. Other mods: Smoothbrain's DualWield (transpilers on EquipItem, ShowHandItems and DoMeleeAttack; prefixes on SetupEquipment, Attack.OnAttackTrigger and ZSyncAnimation.RPC_SetTrigger), DualWielder (EquipItem, UnequipItem and Attack.Start prefixes, VisEquipment.Awake and AttachBackItem postfixes, a Player.SetControls postfix for its swap key), balrond DualMastery (not compatible with other dual wield mods, per its page) and Valheim Ascended's off-hand slot: detect them and stand down. EpicLoot and weapon packs patch Attack; their per-weapon effects would follow m_weapon per hit (unverified). The Weapon revamp edits the same Attack.Start clone (run this one first), the Adrenaline revamp times swings by the weapon's own animation and pays per hit, and MC Crossbow Stays Loaded patches UnequipItem and OnAttackTrigger for reload weapons only (cross test).
- **Related:** Weapon revamp; Adrenaline revamp; Better tower shields
- **Game systems:** [docs/game/combat.md](game/combat.md)
- **Existing mods:** full

An Attack.Start prefix swaps the clone's trigger for the vanilla dualaxes or dual_knives set (any knife in the pair picks the knives) with 4 chain levels, and uses the left weapon for the whole 2nd and 4th steps. - **Inspiration:** Vanilla already has the moves: the Berserkir axes (AxeBerzerkr) and Skoll and Hati (KnifeSkollAndHati) are single items whose attacks play the player's dual-axe and dual-knife animation sets. Blocking without a shield already works, because the left item blocks first (Humanoid.GetCurrentBlocker). What vanilla lacks is an equip rule: Humanoid.EquipItem takes a one-handed weapon out of the left hand. Borrow: DualWielder's vanilla dual triggers on the per-swing clone (no assets, and players without the mod see the same moves), its swap key and its left back joint; balrond DualMastery's off-hand damage factor (50% to 75%) and its secondary that drops the off-hand penalty for more stamina; Smoothbrain's left trails, exclusion list and its rule that spears stay out. What they get wrong: Smoothbrain replaces clips on each client, only pairs weapons of the same skill, scales the items' shared damage during each hit, and its Thunderstore release predates 1.0. DualWielder always sends a second one-handed weapon to the left hand, switches weapons per chain step rather than per hit, keeps the left weapon's own stance, sets 4 chain levels for the knives while the wiki gives Skoll and Hati a 3-hit combo, and its README lists a skill and damage options that the 1.1.2 code no longer has. balrond DualMastery is closed source and adds a skill; Valheim Ascended ties its off-hand slot to an RPG overhaul. Ours, if we build it: any two one-handed swords, axes, clubs or knives, with a key held while equipping to pick the main hand; the vanilla dual stance and triggers read from the vanilla items at runtime; every hit uses the weapon that lands it (damage, status effect, skill, durability) with an off-hand factor; blocking with the off-hand weapon or the better of the two; consistent hide and show, eating, load, death and torch handling; left trails and a second back slot. Technically client-only; it ships as Both with the server's settings for everyone. Or recommend DualWielder, which is maintained on 1.0 and already works without assets.

### New magical items based on valheim magic (ward, heal)

- **Scope:** New · **Feasibility:** medium · **Who needs it:** Both · **Custom assets:** yes · **Status:** idea
- **Approach:** Clone StaffShield, StaffHeal or StaffBlocker. Ward: a friendly Aoe applying a custom SE_Shield variant (absorb scales with skill, optional reflect in the style of SE_React), or a placed ward totem projecting a small dome in the style of ShieldGenerator. Heal: a channelled staff (m_loopingAttack) whose friendly Aoe applies a heal-over-time SE that overrides SetLevel to scale with Blood Magic, paid with health or eitr.
- **Hooks:** `Aoe.Setup`, `Aoe.OnHit`, `Character.RPC_Damage`, `SE_Shield.SetLevel`, `SE_Stats`, `StatusEffect.SetLevel`, `ShieldGenerator.IsInsideShield`, `ShieldGenerator.CheckProjectile`, `Attack.m_loopingAttack`, `ObjectDB.Awake`
- **Risks:** Vanilla already ships StaffHeal, StaffShield and StaffBlocker, so the new items must be clearly different. Custom SEs are applied by hash through hits, so they must be registered in ObjectDB on all clients. Balance vs magic content packs (Therzie Wizardry, EpicLoot).
- **Related:** New summons; Ritual
- **Game systems:** [docs/game/combat.md](game/combat.md)

Built only from vanilla assets; needs Jötunn. Source: github.com/Torvios/ArcaneWards. |

- **Inspiration:** Extend what vanilla already has: SE_Shield (Staff of Protection), the PrivateArea ward and the Ashlands ShieldGenerator dome. Ours, ArcaneWard-style (vanilla assets and mechanics first): ward and heal totems that pulse SE_Shield or heal-over-time to allies, a personal ward charm, cleansing effects, themed through rituals rather than yet another staff pack.

### New summons

- **Scope:** New · **Feasibility:** medium · **Who needs it:** Both · **Custom assets:** yes · **Status:** idea
- **Approach:** Clone the Dead Raiser setup (a staff whose attack projectile is a SpawnAbility, plus Skeleton_Friendly with Tameable as a summon) and swap in cloned or new creatures. Configure m_maxSpawned, m_levelUpSettings (Blood Magic tiers), m_copySkill, m_unsummonDistance and m_levelUpOwnerSkill. Give each summon a role item: a tank that taunts, or a healer using a FriendHurt AI item with a heal Aoe.
- **Hooks:** `SpawnAbility.Spawn`, `SpawnAbility.FindTarget`, `Tameable.Command`, `Tameable.UnsummonMaxInstances`, `Tameable.UpdateSummon`, `MonsterAI.SetFollowTarget`, `SpawnSystem.GetNrOfInstances`, `ZNetScene.Awake`, `ObjectDB.Awake`
- **Risks:** m_maxSpawned counts every loaded instance, including other players' summons (use distinct prefabs or patch the count). Follow and unsummon match on player name. Performance with many summons. Conflicts with taming mods (AllTameable-style) that patch Tameable. New models and animations are needed unless cloning vanilla creatures.
- **Related:** Ritual; New magical items based on valheim magic (ward, heal)

Borrow Cheb's commands and Summon Mastery's scaling and portal following.

### Combat pet

> Animal handling skill that allows you to train tamed animals and bring them in combat with you,  Training and fighting with them makes them gain XP, increasing their stats (up to 5 stars).  Every star they gain, they gain a new skill to use in combat, Naturally spawned starred animals already have the abilities

- **Scope:** New · **Feasibility:** hard · **Who needs it:** Both · **Custom assets:** no · **Status:** idea
- **Approach:** Stars are the vanilla creature level (ZDO level, 1 = no star, 6 = 5 stars). Character.SetLevel has no owner check, so the mod calls it on the pet's ZDO owner; it rescales max HP (Character.SetupMaxHealth = base x level), and damage follows the level through Attack.GetLevelDamageFactor (1 + 0.5 per extra level). Wild animals keep their spawn level when tamed, so abilities keyed on GetLevel() give natural 1-2 star animals their abilities for free. Pet XP is a custom ZDO float written only by the pet's owner. It comes from three sources: a Character.RaiseSkill postfix (a tame's own hits call it); a kill bonus from a Character.OnDeath postfix, sent to the pet's owner through an RPC registered in Tameable.Awake; and reduced, capped XP for sparring with training dummies (the tamed branch of BaseAI.IsEnemy makes tames and dummies enemies, but BaseAI.FindEnemy skips m_aiSkipTarget characters and the dummy prefab sets that flag, unverified: third-party 1.0 dump; so a tame never picks a dummy itself: sparring starts when a dummy attacks the tame, which fights back through MonsterAI.OnDamaged when it has no target yet, and in the Training dummies revamp an ON dummy still attacks tames, as in vanilla, while an OFF one attacks nothing). A new Animal Handling skill (Jotunn SkillManager, or patches on Skills.IsSkillValid and Skills.GetSkillDef) rises on the handler's own client through an RPC. It gates the star cap and adds damage through the vanilla ZDO float RandomSkillFactor, which Character.GetRandomSkillFactor multiplies into the melee, area and projectile hits (Attack) of a non-player character. Active abilities are cloned attack items added in a Humanoid.GiveDefaultItems postfix, and added directly on level-up. Humanoid.EquipBestWeapon, called about once a second while the pet has a target, picks them by range, interval and AiTarget, and they reuse each animal's own attacks (wolf, boar, lox, asksvin, moose). Passive abilities are status effects that the owner re-applies. Patches on EnemyHud.ShowHud/UpdateHuds and LevelEffects.SetupLevelVisualization show stars 3-5. The mod sets m_commandable in ZNetScene.Awake on every combat species whose prefab lacks it, and a Procreation.Procreate patch clamps offspring (newborns and eggs) to the natural maximum level.
- **Hooks:** `Character.SetLevel`, `Character.SetupMaxHealth`, `Attack.GetLevelDamageFactor`, `Character.RaiseSkill`, `Character.OnDeath`, `Character.GetRandomSkillFactor`, `Tameable.Awake`, `Tameable.GetHoverText`, `Tameable.Interact`, `Tameable.RPC_Command`, `Humanoid.GiveDefaultItems`, `Humanoid.EquipBestWeapon`, `BaseAI.CanUseAttack`, `BaseAI.IsEnemy`, `BaseAI.FindEnemy`, `SEMan.AddStatusEffect`, `Skills.IsSkillValid`, `Skills.GetSkillDef`, `EnemyHud.ShowHud`, `EnemyHud.UpdateHuds`, `LevelEffects.SetupLevelVisualization`, `Procreation.Procreate`, `ZNetScene.Awake`, `ObjectDB.Awake`
- **Risks:** Non-owner clients read Character.m_level from the ZDO only in Character.Awake. A mid-session star-up must therefore be broadcast (RPC or ZDO poll), and vanilla clients keep the old level until the pet reloads. That includes its damage if a vanilla client takes ownership. The pet's AI owner is not necessarily its handler, and kills resolve on the victim's owner, so every XP path needs an RPC (BetterTames silently skips that case). Custom skills are dropped on load when the mod is removed (Skills.IsSkillValid). Vanilla scaling at 5 stars is 6x HP and 3.5x damage, and flattening it only works where the pet's owner has the mod. The RandomSkillFactor bonus does not reach AoE payloads (a non-player Aoe ignores it). Support (FriendHurt) abilities are only picked while the pet has an enemy target. Death stays permanent (Tameable.OnDeath only drops the saddle). About 25 abilities (5 stars x wolf, boar, lox, asksvin, moose) need balancing and must use animation triggers each Animator already has. The hen has no attack-like asset, so it probably cannot fight. Which vanilla tames are commandable is prefab data, and sources disagree about lox. Conflicts: StarLevelSystem/CLLC/ReefLevels (level scaling and star UI), BetterTames and Monster Level Up (their own kill level-ups), BetterTames, GrindstoneSkills, ReefLevels and our Breeding revamp (offspring stars), and BeastMaster/AllTameable/LetMeTameYou (Tameable patches).
- **Related:** Breeding revamp; Training dummies revamp; More training dummies; New summons; New ability depending on skill level; Bring tamed / pets through stone portal; Bring tamed / pets on ship; Ping system revamp
- **Game systems:** [docs/game/combat.md](game/combat.md)
- **Existing mods:** partial

Tames are touched only at birth: newborns draw stars around the parents' average (up to 4). No pet progression, but prior art for showing 3-5 stars, and a conflict on offspring level. Replaces the creature part of CLLC. Server and every client. |
  | [WolfPack (Neobotics)](https://thunderstore.io/c/valheim/p/Neobotics/WolfPack/) | v1.2.0, updated 2026-09-11 (1.0: yes, changelog 'Updated for Valheim 1.0') | Hotkeys make all nearby tames of a selected kind follow or stay. Also commands tames to attack the hovered target, spreads followers around the player and caps the number of followers. Server-authoritative config. No progression. Also on Nexus (mods/1549). |
  | [Cartur's Follow Command (Cartur)](https://thunderstore.io/c/valheim/p/Cartur/Carturs_Follow_Command/) | v1.0.2, updated 2026-09-21 (1.0: likely), AI-generated | Turns on Tameable.m_commandable for every tame, so boars, hens and asksvin follow like wolves and lox (the vanilla followers, per its page; the Valheim wiki's Taming page names only wolves). Uses the vanilla follow system, so the follow state survives relogs. Source on GitHub (jekkle/cartur-follow-command). |
  | [Wolf Commands (Fai)](https://thunderstore.io/c/valheim/p/Fai/Wolf_Commands/) | v1.0.0, updated 2026-09-22 (1.0: yes), AI-generated | G sends following tames forward for 3 s to engage. H recalls them and clears their alert state. Saves nothing in world data. |
  | [FeedLikeGrandma (sighsorry)](https://thunderstore.io/c/valheim/p/sighsorry/FeedLikeGrandma/) | v1.1.3, updated 2026-09-09, targets Valheim 1.0.7 (1.0: yes, Deep North Update tag), AI Generated tag | Adult tames level up by being hand-fed (level progress per food), capped at level 3 by default (configurable). Also mounted stamina settings (attack and jump costs), feed barrels and taming orbs. No combat progression. Source on GitHub (sighsorry1029/FeedLikeGrandma). |
  | [ValheimCompanion (PranaySPatil)](https://github.com/PranaySPatil/ValheimCompanion) | no release, last push 2026-05-13 (1.0: unknown), GitHub only | Prototype of an owner-bound wolf companion with Follow / Stay / Aggressive / Defensive orders, a fixed configurable star level, and death modes (permadeath, downed and revivable, recoverable). Single-player only, no XP and no abilities; phase 1 of a planned 6-phase roadmap. BepInEx + Jotunn. |

- **Inspiration:** Every piece exists somewhere, but never all together, and nobody ties combat abilities to stars. BetterTames and Monster Level Up roll a star on kills. BetterTames caps it at 2 stars and loses the level-up when the victim and the tame have different ZDO owners. TrollCompanion gives summoned trolls stars at kill thresholds (25 and 75 kills), shares kill credit within 25 m and shows progress in a panel. ValBall and BeastMaster grow hidden stat multipliers instead of stars (BeastMaster's 10 training levels are explicitly not stars), so other players cannot see the progress. GrindstoneSkills and BeastMaster's Taming Mastery show that a player skill can carry pet bonuses. ReefLevels already draws up to five stars in the nameplate. Borrow: ValBall's XP weighting by biome, victim stars and bosses; TrollCompanion's visible thresholds and shared kill credit; BeastMaster's 'training while fed and near the owner'; WolfPack's attack-the-hovered-target command; Cartur's m_commandable switch. Ours differs in these ways. The stars are the vanilla creature level, which is visible, synced, already scales HP and damage, and means natural 1-2 star animals arrive with their abilities. Each species (including the Deep North moose) gets one ability per star, built from its own attacks. XP is routed to the pet's ZDO owner by RPC. A handler skill gates the star cap and adds damage through the vanilla RandomSkillFactor. Star UI goes up to 5 stars. Offspring are clamped, so breeding cannot copy trained stars. When StarLevelSystem or ReefLevels is installed, leave star display to it rather than drawing a second set.

### Ritual

> (consuming items, requiring multiple people)

- **Scope:** New · **Feasibility:** hard · **Who needs it:** Both · **Custom assets:** yes · **Status:** idea
- **Approach:** Build a custom altar piece with a ZNetView component. Players join via Interact; the owner stores participant IDs and join times in the ZDO. Players offer items: the owner validates, increments ZDO counters and replies with a confirmation RPC so the offering client removes the items (OfferingBowl/Feast pattern). Once N participants stay within the radius for the channel duration, the owner resolves: SEs by hash to participants, a boss or event spawn, or a reward drop.
- **Hooks:** `OfferingBowl.UseItem`, `OfferingBowl.RPC_SpawnBoss`, `Feast.RPC_TryEat`, `Player.GetPlayersInRange`, `SEMan.AddStatusEffect`, `ZNetView.Register`, `SpawnAbility`, `ObjectDB.Awake`
- **Risks:** Ownership can change mid-ritual, so all state must live in the ZDO. Item dupes are possible if removal is not confirmed. Version mismatches between players. Needs assets (altar, VFX). Overlaps with Crafting and Building design.
- **Related:** New summons; New magical items based on valheim magic (ward, heal)
- **Game systems:** [docs/game/combat.md](game/combat.md)
- **Existing mods:** none

  | Mod | Status | Notes |
  |---|---|---|
  | [Krumpac Sacrificing Altars](https://thunderstore.io/c/valheim/p/Krumpac/Krumpac_Sacrificing_Altars/) | v7.3.2, 2025-09-07, deprecated, needs registration (dead) | God altars, a praying skill, sacrifices for blessings. Single player. |
  | [CustomizeAltars (Huntardys)](https://thunderstore.io/c/valheim/p/Huntardys/CustomizeAltars/) | v2.0.0, 2024-02, deprecated (dead) | JSON config for boss altar items and spawns. |
  | [Vikings Summoner (Radamanto)](https://thunderstore.io/c/valheim/p/Radamanto/Vikings_Summoner/) | v1.5.1, 2026-09-26 (1.0: likely) | Ritual artifacts and summoning totems are flavour only; one player uses an item. |
  | [RtDMonsters (Soloredis)](https://thunderstore.io/c/valheim/p/Soloredis/RtDMonsters/) | v2.4.14, 2026-05, deprecated (dead) | Summon custom bosses through altar items. |

- **Inspiration:** No mod requires several participants. Vanilla building blocks: OfferingBowl (consume items, spawn), Incinerator (consume, produce), ShieldGenerator and PrivateArea (area effects). Ours: a ritual circle with N runestones that players must interact with at the same time, synchronized through the ZDO. It consumes offerings and triggers summons, weather, area buffs, crop growth or a raid. A strong co-op hook and a cross-category magic theme.
